Ronaldo dà l'addio alla Nazionale: "Riconsegno la maglia numero 7. Per sempre sarà..."

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ronaldo dà l'addio alla Nazionale: "Riconsegno la maglia numero 7. Per sempre sarà..."

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Dopo 23 anni, Cristiano Ronaldo riconsegna la maglia della Nazionale portoghese. Con un video emozionante, la musica di Morricone a sottolineare le sue parole, il calciatore portoghese - 234 gare e segnato 146 reti con la maglia della sua Nazione, un Europeo e due Nations League vinte - legge la sua lettera per i tifosi e per i suoi connazionali.

«È arrivato il giorno di restituire la maglia numero 7 della Nazionale di tutti noi. Non l'ho mai sentita come qualcosa di esclusivamente mio. Ma per 23 anni ho avuto il privilegio e la responsabilità di indossarla, difenderla e portarla con me negli stadi di tutto il mondo. Oggi è arrivato il momento di consegnarla al futuro. Non è stata una decisione facile, perché ci sono decisioni che non si prendono soltanto con la testa, ma anche con il cuore. Si conclude un percorso iniziato nell'agosto del 2003 e che ha cambiato per sempre la mia vita», ha detto il numero 7.

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

«Quando guardo indietro e penso a tutto quello che ho vissuto con la Nazionale, non vedo soltanto partite, gol, record o trofei. Vedo persone. Vedo gli emigrati che per 90 minuti si sentivano un po' più vicini al Portogallo. Vedo milioni di portoghesi che non ho mai conosciuto personalmente, ma che hanno fatto parte della mia vita. Perché, in fondo, il calcio è molto più di un gioco. Il calcio sono le persone. Durante tutta la mia carriera in Nazionale ho cercato di superare i miei limiti. Ho voluto vincere, essere il migliore, fare la storia. Sono sempre stato esigente con me stesso. Ho sempre voluto di più. Ho sempre creduto che potessimo arrivare più lontano. E ho dato tutto quello che avevo per questa maglia», ha continuato.

�Ci sono stati pomeriggi e serate perfette, vittorie che non dimenticherò mai, sconfitte che ancora oggi faccio fatica a ricordare. Ho commesso anche degli errori, ho sofferto e ci sono stati momenti in cui non sono riuscito a dare ai portoghesi ciò che desideravano tanto. Ma non mi sono mai arreso e, soprattutto, non ho mai smesso di provare orgoglio nel rappresentare il Portogallo. In tutti questi anni ho imparato cose molto semplici. I gol passano, le coppe restano nei musei, i record prima o poi vengono battuti. Ma c'è qualcosa che non si può mai cancellare: il posto che occupiamo nel cuore delle persone», ha aggiunto riferendosi a tutto l'amore che il popolo portoghese gli ha sempre dato. Come tutti i bambini che vanno a spasso per il mondo con a maglia numero 7 del Portogallo e imitano la sua esultanza.

�Se anche solo una volta vi ho reso felici, se anche solo una volta un mio gol vi ha fatto abbracciare qualcuno, dimenticare una preoccupazione o provare orgoglio per il nostro Paese, allora ne è valsa la pena. Perché essere portoghesi significa anche questo: soffriamo insieme, ci rialziamo insieme. Torniamo a credere. Il Portogallo avrà sempre bisogno di giovani che sognano, di giocatori che non si arrendono mai, di nuove generazioni che credono sia possibile arrivare dove nessuno è mai arrivato. Perché un Paese che continua a creare nuovi eroi è un Paese che non smette mai di credere in se stesso».

«Alla mia famiglia, grazie per aver condiviso con me questo sogno. Grazie per aver compreso le assenze, i sacrifici e i momenti in cui la Nazionale ha dovuto occupare uno spazio che apparteneva anche a voi. Se sono arrivato così lontano è perché siamo sempre stati mano nella mano. E ai portoghesi, a tutti i portoghesi sparsi per il mondo, voglio dire un'ultima cosa: c'è un'immagine che porterò sempre con me, il momento in cui inizia l'inno nazionale».

�La maglia numero 7 resta. L'orgoglio di averla indossata sarà mio per sempre. Il Portogallo sarà sempre dentro di me. Per sempre, Cristiano Ronaldo».

Testata giornalistica registrata - Aut.Trib.di Bologna n. 8183 del 17/05/2011 AMALA S.N.C. - Direttore Responsabile: Daniele Mari e Giovanni Montopoli - Vice Direttore : Sabine Bertagna Il sito FCINTER1908 di titolarità di AMALA S.N.C. con sede in Bologna Via del Pratello, 27 C.F./PI e numero di iscrizione al Reg. Como Detectar e Neutralizar Deepfakes nas Eleições 2026

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A corrida eleitoral está a todo vapor e, a cada clique, milhões de usuários pesquisam “deepfake eleitoral 2026” no Google Trends. Vídeos hiper‑realistas, vozes sintéticas e a velocidade de disseminação no TikTok, YouTube e WhatsApp transformaram a verificação de conteúdo em uma missão urgente para eleitores, jornalistas e formuladores de políticas.

Neste guia prático (≈ 2 800 palavras) você vai entender a evolução dos deepfakes de vídeo e áudio em 2026, conhecer casos reais de manipulação nas eleições dos EUA, Índia e Brasil, e receber um pipeline de detecção em tempo real usando apenas ferramentas open‑source e APIs gratuitas. No final, você terá scripts prontos, um checklist de verificação e uma tabela comparativa de precisão × custo dos principais modelos — tudo para identificar e denunciar conteúdos falsos antes que eles influenciem o voto.

Dica prática: se o vídeo contém blink irregular ou sincronização labial “fora do tempo”, pode ser um sinal, mas modelos de 2026 já corrigem esses artefatos.

Interpretando o score: valores > 0,80 indicam alta probabilidade de manipulação; recomenda‑se revisão humana imediata.

Se 3 ou mais itens apontarem risco, classifique como “ALTO” e reporte ao Fact‑Checking Hub da sua região.

Os próximos 90 dias serão decisivos para a democracia de três potências. A combinação de vídeos hiper‑realistas, vozes sintéticas e a velocidade de compartilhamento exige uma resposta rápida e baseada em ferramentas acessíveis. Ao seguir o pipeline apresentado, usar o checklist de 5 minutos e divulgar denúncias, qualquer pessoa — seja jornalista, cidadão ou desenvolvedor — pode neutralizar a maioria das campanhas de desinformação antes que elas alcancem o eleitorado.

Ação imediata: clone o repositório, execute o container Docker e teste com um vídeo suspeito hoje mesmo. Cada verificação bem‑sucedida reduz a margem de erro dos deepfakes e fortalece a integridade das eleições de 2026.

Browser Location Access: What It Actually Shares and How to Control It

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

When a website asks "Allow location access?" — most users either click Allow reflexively or Block without fully understanding what they're granting. Here's what browser location access actually means, what extensions can do with it, and how to make deliberate choices.

The browser's Geolocation API lets websites request your location. When a site calls navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition(), the browser shows a permission prompt. If you grant it, the browser returns a latitude/longitude coordinate to the page.

On desktop: Not GPS. Desktop browsers use a combination of:

The accuracy on desktop varies from neighborhood-level (Wi-Fi triangulation) to city-level (IP-only). It's not your exact address.

On mobile: GPS is used if available, which is accurate to meters. Mobile location requests are much more precise than desktop.

When you grant location access to a site, the site receives:

That's it — no street address, no "home address," no place names. The website has to reverse geocode the coordinates to get a named location, which is an additional step they may or may not do.

Your IP address is always visible to sites, regardless of location permission. Many sites geolocate from IP before even requesting the Geolocation API.

Browser extensions are different from websites for location access. Extensions don't use the Geolocation API the same way — they have access to browser APIs that websites don't.

What extensions typically do with location: Most legitimate location-related extensions (weather, map, local search) request location to provide their core function — a weather extension needs your location to show weather. This is expected and appropriate.

Red flags: An extension that requests location access but whose stated purpose has nothing to do with location is worth scrutinizing.

Weather & Clock Dashboard for Firefox uses your location to show the weather for your area. The permissions requested are specifically for location and the ability to fetch weather data from an API (Open-Meteo).

The extension doesn't send your location to its own servers. It passes your coordinates to Open-Meteo (the weather data provider) to get forecast data. Open-Meteo is a public API with an open-data license; it doesn't have accounts or persistent tracking.

For users who don't want to share location even with the extension: you can enter a manual location by city name instead, and the extension will use that. No location permission required.

Firefox gives you granular control over location access:

Per-site management: Settings → Privacy & Security → Permissions → Location → Settings. This shows every site that has asked for location access and what you answered. You can change individual site permissions here without affecting others.

Default behavior: You can set Firefox to block all location requests by default, prompt case-by-case (the default), or allow all. Settings → Privacy & Security → Permissions → Location → check "Block new requests asking to access your location" to default to block.

Incognito/private windows: Location permissions granted in a private window don't persist. The site has to ask again next time.

Allow location for: mapping/navigation sites (Google Maps, OpenStreetMap), weather services, local search, delivery sites where location saves you typing your address.

Consider blocking for: news sites, social media platforms, general-purpose shopping sites, sites where your precise location has no clear functional use.

Always review when: a site you don't recognize or haven't used before asks for location, or a site asks for location but you can't figure out why they'd need it.

The permission prompt is there for a reason. Using it deliberately — not reflexively clicking Allow or Block — is the main privacy practice worth building.

Firefox's default geolocation provider is different from Chrome's. Firefox defaults to routing geolocation queries through Mozilla's geolocation service (which uses Google Location Services under the hood but via Mozilla), which provides some additional privacy layer compared to Chrome's direct Google use.

For higher privacy: about:config → geo.provider.network.url can be changed to alternative providers, or geolocation can be disabled entirely by setting geo.enabled to false. This disables the API completely — all location requests will appear to fail to sites.

The practical privacy tradeoff: disabling geolocation entirely means weather extensions, map sites, and local search can't use your location, which is often the reason you're visiting them. The per-site permission model is a better balance for most users than a global block.

Best Firefox Extensions of 2026: The Short List That Actually Matters

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Firefox extension library has hundreds of thousands of entries. Most aren't worth installing. These are the ones that actually make a measurable difference to daily browsing — tested, stable, and genuinely useful.

Install it, leave the default settings on, and forget about it. Advanced mode is available for users who want per-domain blocking control, but the defaults are good.

Who it's for: Anyone who mixes work and personal browsing, developers who test multiple user states, privacy-conscious users.

This is a Firefox-only feature. Chrome doesn't have an equivalent.

What it does: Password manager. Generates, stores, and autofills passwords across devices.

Why not Firefox's built-in: Firefox's built-in password manager is good for most users. Bitwarden adds encrypted vault syncing across all devices (including mobile, other browsers), sharing with family/team, and advanced features like TOTP generation. It's also fully open-source.

Who it's for: Users who access accounts from multiple devices or browsers, teams that need to share credentials.

Free tier is excellent. The paid plan ($10/year) adds emergency access and advanced 2FA.

What it does: Moves your tabs to a vertical sidebar with hierarchical tree structure.

Why it matters: For users who routinely have 20-50+ tabs open, the horizontal tab bar breaks down completely. Tree Style Tab lets you see tab titles in full, groups related tabs under parent pages, and collapses branches when not needed.

Who it's for: Researchers, developers, writers, anyone who maintains many concurrent tab sessions.

The learning curve is real — takes a week to build the habit. The payoff is proportional to how many tabs you typically have open.

What it does: Makes GitHub significantly more usable. Adds PR status indicators, cleaner diff displays, keyboard shortcuts, one-click file navigation, reaction counts on PRs, and dozens of small quality-of-life improvements.

Who it's for: Anyone who uses GitHub regularly for code review, project tracking, or contribution.

The number of small paper cuts it removes is hard to quantify until you use it.

What it does: Learns which domains track you across sites based on observed behavior and blocks them.

Why use it with uBlock Origin: uBlock Origin uses pre-built blocklists. Privacy Badger adds behavioral analysis to catch trackers that aren't on those lists. They complement each other without significant overlap.

Who it's for: Users who care about tracking protection and want a heuristic layer on top of list-based blocking.

What it does: Replaces the default new tab page with time, date, weather forecast, and world clocks.

Why it matters: The default Firefox new tab has a grid of promoted sites and (in some configurations) news/stories content. Neither is useful if you open a new tab to navigate or search. A minimal new tab that shows ambient information — time and weather — without pull content is a better baseline for most workflows.

Weather & Clock Dashboard is the Firefox add-on for this. It shows the current conditions and 3-day forecast using Open-Meteo weather data, plus configurable world clocks for time zone awareness. No feed, no news, no attention design.

Who it's for: Anyone who finds the default new tab distracting, users who check weather during work, remote workers coordinating across time zones.

What it does: Automatically skips sponsored segments, self-promotion, and outros in YouTube videos.

Why it's notable: It's community-crowdsourced — users mark segments in videos, the marks are shared, and the extension skips them for everyone. It works astonishingly well for popular content.

Who it's for: Anyone who watches YouTube regularly and finds the mid-video ads more disruptive than pre-roll.

More extensions = more attack surface and more performance overhead. The list above is the floor of what's worth having for most users, not a shopping cart to fill. Install what solves an actual problem you have; don't install speculatively.

The highest-ROI starting set for most people: uBlock Origin + Containers + a password manager + Weather & Clock Dashboard. That's four extensions, ten minutes to set up, and a meaningfully better browser experience from day one.

No PhD in computer vision required.

Starting from Abbottabad: What Your Hometown Decides — and What It Doesn't

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I am from Abbottabad, Pakistan. It is a small city in the hills, and it is home. Everything I have built — Buzz Capitol, Digi Skill Pro Academy, findservicenest.com, more than fifteen YouTube channels, six books — started there. People sometimes ask me whether starting from a small town held me back. The honest answer is: it decided some things, and it did not decide others. Knowing the difference matters more than the town itself.

Your hometown decides your starting point. It decides what is around you when you are young: the schools, the people you meet, the examples of what a working life can look like. In a small city, you see fewer big companies and fewer obvious paths into media or technology. That is real, and I will not pretend it is not. When I started, there was no local roadmap for what I wanted to do. I had to find the road myself, mostly online.

But your hometown does not decide your reach. The internet changed that equation completely, and it changed it for everyone at once. A student in Abbottabad can take the same free class as a student anywhere else. A reader in another country does not know or care where an article was written. A client hiring a service does not ask for your address before asking whether you can do the job. The work travels; the writer stays put.

That is the first thing my hometown taught me: place is a starting point, not a limit. It taught me to be resourceful, because when the thing you need is not nearby, you learn to find it online, to teach yourself, to ask strangers for help and to help strangers in return. Some of the most useful skills I have came from that habit — looking for answers instead of waiting for them to arrive.

The second thing it taught me is patience. In a small city, things move at their own pace. People know each other. Reputation travels by word of mouth, and it travels slowly — but it also lasts. That shaped how I think about building anything. I did not chase a quick splash. I built steadily, in my own name, one article and one video and one class at a time, because that is how trust works where I come from: you show up, you do the work, and over time people believe you.

There is a part of this story I want to be careful about. I am not saying that where you are from does not matter at all. It matters. It shapes your accent, your manners, your sense of what is possible, and the problems you understand first-hand. I understand what it means to start with limited resources, because I lived it. That understanding is part of why I made Digi Skill Pro Academy's classes completely free. In 2025, more than ten thousand students joined, and more than five thousand of them reported that they started earning. I do not claim those numbers as a triumph of mine; I mention them because they are the reason the academy exists — to give people starting from places like mine a way in.

Starting from Abbottabad also taught me something about independence. When you are far from the centres where decisions get made, you learn to make your own decisions. Buzz Capitol is an independent news and media platform because I wanted it to be free to say what it thinks. That independence was not a strategy I read in a book. It was a habit I learned from building things on my own terms, in a place where nobody was going to build them for me.

I still live and work from here, and I do not see that as a compromise. The world comes to my work through a screen, and my work goes out to the world the same way. My channels reach more than ten million followers across platforms. My books reach readers I will never meet. None of that required me to leave. It required me to stay, and to build something worth finding.

If you are starting from a small town, here is what I would say. Do not waste energy resenting your starting point. Use it. The constraints of a small place teach you to be self-reliant, and self-reliance is the most portable skill there is. Start with what you can do today, with the connection you have. Publish your work under your own name. Let the internet do what it is good at: carrying good work further than its author's address.

Your hometown decides where you begin. It does not decide how far your work can travel. That part is yours.

Sami Ullah is the Founder, CEO and Editor-in-Chief of Buzz Capitol, Chairman of Digi Skill Pro Academy, and author of six books. He lives and works in Abbottabad, Pakistan.

SERP Data Providers Say Google Is Throttling Goto Result Links via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

SERP data providers say Google throttles goto links per results page, so some rank data may store Google redirect links in place of page URLs.

Google is restricting the speed at which anyone can follow (google.com/goto) redirects for search results on each page, as per DataForSEO and Serper. Both sell Google search result API access. Due to this restriction rank data provided by these services may contain Google redirect URLs instead of actual URLs.

According to DataForSEO, the restrictions mean that they have seen some of their request modes only resolving part of the destination URLs. Serper’s status page first noted the issue at 1:30 a.m. UTC on October 30. SerpApi, another seller of search data, also logged goto links for organic results on the same date. Neither DataForSEO nor Serper links to a Google announcement regarding the restriction and the statements below are from their websites.

DataForSEO stated in its November 2nd article that when you open multiple links from the same results page in rapid succession, Google will begin to throttle the redirect(s). Each link carries an identifier based on the results page it originated from.

When Google throttles your redirects, instead of sending your requests for resolution directly to the destination, Google will send back a page with a “Continue” button. That button will only become available after a waiting period. The amount of time the continue button is disabled grows the faster the previous links were resolved.

For example, take a results page that contains 18 goto links to other websites. If all 18 links are requested in quick succession, Google may allow the first 5-6 to be resolved immediately. However, the 7th link may result in a 1 second wait before being able to access the website. The 8th link may then take 3 seconds. And this process continues until the 15th link, where there could be a 40 second wait before accessing that website. DataForSEO reported that they had instances of waiting up to 120 seconds for 1 URL.

The limit is based on the results page and not based on the IP address or the search query used. Also, DataForSEO reports that simply switching IP addresses or search queries will not help bypass this limitation. Additionally, it says that even if you wait an hour or longer before resolving all of the links, Google still uses the same results page limits.

DataForSEO’s Standard Priority mode now resolves all goto URLs, although the added delays may mean execution takes longer. In High Priority and Live modes, the company says about half of goto URLs are resolved.

Serper’s status page says Google “limits how many /goto result links can be followed per results page.” Its first fix resolved the top three organic links and returned results four through 10 as goto links, which Serper said work about 30 seconds after the search. It added a /resolve endpoint to fetch the rest.

On Oct. 31, Serper said it had found another way to get the real URLs and that all result links had them again. It called that “likely temporary.”

SerpApi’s Status Page displays an October 30th event indicating that its Google Search API returned goto links instead of regular links for organic search results; it states that they had the issue fixed by November 2nd. However, there is a second incident reported on November 3rd reporting intermittent issues with the same problem. Neither incident gives a cause.

DataForSEO dates Google’s move to www.google.com/goto links in Search Results to late August. It says the links cannot be decoded, and therefore a tool will have to follow each link to find out where it goes. The link-following part is what the new limit slows down.

Some rank tracking services as well as in house reports using landing pages for URL-level reporting may display Google goto redirects (google.com/goto) in place of true destination URLs from approximately October 30th onward based upon the service provider and how the requests were made. If a specific URL is missing from a URL-level report it could still be showing up in a ranking position and have an unresolved redirect at the URL location. Prior to reporting a URL-level loss check whether the stored URL is a Google redirect, also contact your service provider and determine what method was used to collect the data.

As of my check on Nov. 4, SerpApi’s Incident Report on goto links from Nov. 3 is still listed as Identified. Serper states that in the event that Google also puts a cap on the method it uses for retrieving actual URLs, it would revert to automatically resolving the top three results and use /resolve to fetch the remaining after approximately thirty seconds. DataForSEO stated it will continue to adjust its processing based on what it observes regarding how Google deals with goto resolutions.

Reports covering the last few days of October through early November should be reviewed again at some point when providers have determined how they will handle goto resolution.

Follow on YouTube. Matt G. Southern is the Senior News Writer at Search Engine Journal, where he’s covered Google, SEO, ...

Nvidia Investors Need to Be Paying Attention on Nov. 17

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Mark your calendar. Nvidia is on the verge of a business update that will be incredibly important for the stock market.

Nvidia (NVDA -3.17%) is on the verge of a crucial moment. On Nov. 17, it will publish its third-quarter earnings results, and this event has huge implications for the whole stock market.

As the leading provider of the high-powered processing hardware used to train and power today's most advanced artificial intelligence (AI) models, Nvidia has become the single most important company to watch when it comes to the health of both the AI industry and the broader market. That means that its upcoming report could spur substantial movement in the company's share price, and could also have a reverberating impact on many other holdings in investors' portfolios.

Stock valuations in the AI industry have experienced an incredible run-up, but investors have recently been questioning the sustainability of their growth-dependent valuations. Along with macroeconomic dynamics, the market is wavering on AI-specific dynamics, including monetization efficiency, financing, and electricity sourcing. Nvidia's stock price has pulled back as these concerns have intensified, and it's now down roughly 13.5% from the high that it reached last month. In this environment, the company's upcoming report is poised to be an important valuation test.

With the Q2 report that it published at the end of August, Nvidia issued strong guidance for the third quarter. Sales were forecast to grow by roughly 106% year over year to approximately $108 billion. The company also said that it expected a non-GAAP (adjusted) gross margin of roughly 74%.

Nvidia's history of big earnings and revenue beats is creating high expectations for its third-quarter report. Simply hitting the company's guidance targets or equaling the average Wall Street analyst estimates would probably provoke a bearish response. Investors are looking for the company to deliver strong indications that demand for advanced AI processors and related technologies remains sky-high. With AI stocks faltering recently, Nov. 17 won't just be an important date to watch for Nvidia investors -- it will be an important day in determining where the stock market heads next.

He Trolls AI Labs for a Living. Now They All Want to Work With Him

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

OpenAI, Anthropic, and xAI have all asked creator Matt Husk to collaborate following the viral success of his videos trolling AI models.

During the WIRED World Fair on Wednesday, Husk told the crowd “all the big AI companies” have inquired about working with him, including Elon Musk’s xAI, which Husk claimed offered a Tesla for a week if he made videos exclusively for the firm.

The comedian has gained some 2 million followers combined across Instagram, TikTok, and YouTube, as well as a deal with the talent agency Unicorn, through videoing himself making deadpan requests to ChatGPT’s voice model—asking it to count to 100, or to beatbox, for example. His viral videos have exposed the weaknesses of OpenAI’s LLM, which comically botches the tasks and swings between sycophancy and gaslighting Husk, and earned him the title of “Sam Altman’s worst nightmare.”

In April, Altman, nodding and smiling, admitted on tech podcast Mostly Human that the model’s inability to set an accurate timer was a “known issue” and would be for another year, after being shown a Husk video in which he asked ChatGPT to time him running a mile and found that it measured a span of a few seconds as lasting “around 10 minutes.” When Husk says “I swear I was faster than that,” it replies with a sympathetic tone: “It can feel faster in the moment.”

During his interview at WIRED World Fair with Shell Game podcast host Evan Ratliff, Husk said OpenAI offered him early access to a patch for the timer issue if he shared his account. He declined. The company had previously emailed Husk asking to collaborate on a video. “They offered me the smallest amount,” he said. “I was just like, ‘No.’” The firm then offered to send him gifts and asked for his home address; Again, Husk declined.

OpenAI, Anthropic, and xAI (which was recently rolled into SpaceX to create SpaceXAI and then SpaceXSI after President Trump renamed artificial intelligence “super intelligence”) did not respond to requests for comment.

AI labs are increasingly pitching their products as capable of handling everyday chores. On Tuesday, OpenAI announced its model had scored “very highly” on a benchmark built to model its ability to help people in their personal lives, such as meal prep and enrolling a child in kindergarten. “The way they promote it is like ‘It can do anything,’” Husk said, “so I’ll put it to the test—see if it can order bread for me.” In one of his videos, Husk asks AI to help him negotiate some bread on Craigslist down from a starting price of $10,000 to $5. The model accepts a price of $400.

Husk began publicly trolling chatbots about a year ago. He told Ratliff, a WIRED contributor, at the time he liked to make TikToks asking people questions, but there weren’t many around while he was working his landscaping job, and he ran out of ideas. So he recorded himself telling ChatGPT a nuclear bomb had gone off. The model’s response, “I’m rooting for you,” and advice to take shelter “was not helpful at all,” he said. “I thought it was a pretty bad video,” he told Ratliff, “and the next morning I had 100,00 views, and I was like, ‘Let’s tell it I got hit by a train.’”

Since then, Husk has secured brand partnerships with CeraVe and Ninja. He also has an upcoming original series with The Onion, where he learns complicated skills, like being a mime, over Zoom. “I'm spending too much time talking to AI,” Husk explained, “so I'm going to branch out and learn stuff from humans.”

While Husk finds himself talking to AI a lot to scope out ideas for videos, “I don't really use it in that creative sense,” such as to generate ideas, he said, because they would all be “too similar.” “I'd rather just have my worst idea at least be my idea rather than AI's idea for a video,” he said.

La famiglia di De Miguel ringrazia per la vicinanza: "Ora abbiamo bisogno di serenità"

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

La famiglia di Jesus de Miguel ha deciso di rompere il silenzio con un commosso comunicato per ringraziare tutti coloro che hanno manifestato vicinanza dopo la tragica scomparsa del calciatore. Attraverso una nota diffusa dal rappresentante del giocatore, Manuel Hernández, i familiari hanno espresso profonda gratitudine per i messaggi, le telefonate e le numerose dimostrazioni di affetto ricevute dal mondo del calcio e non solo: "Non abbiamo parole sufficienti per ringraziare per ogni messaggio, ogni chiamata e ogni gesto di affetto che ci sta arrivando", si legge nel comunicato. Un pensiero rivolto a compagni di squadra, amici, tifosi e club che hanno voluto far sentire la propria vicinanza in un momento di enorme dolore.

La famiglia ha poi sottolineato quanto sia stato importante scoprire l'impronta lasciata da Jesús nella vita di tante persone: "Sentire così tanto affetto nei suoi confronti e rendersi conto del segno che ha lasciato in così tante persone è qualcosa che porteremo sempre con noi". Accanto ai ringraziamenti, arriva anche un appello al rispetto della sfera privata. I familiari chiedono infatti che venga tutelata la loro intimità e quella di tutte le persone che li stanno accompagnando in questi giorni difficili. "Abbiamo bisogno di vivere questo lutto con la serenità che una situazione del genere richiede", spiegano, pur ribadendo la riconoscenza per l'ondata di vicinanza ricevuta.

Il messaggio si chiude con un ricordo carico di emozione: "Jesús lascia un vuoto impossibile da colmare, ma anche un ricordo immenso in tutti coloro che hanno avuto la fortuna di condividere un tratto di vita con lui. Grazie per avergli voluto bene, per ricordarlo e per esserci vicini".

The Last Of Us Part 2 DLC Officially Released

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Last of Us has done a remarkable job of remaining relevant as a franchise without any new releases confirmed, especially considering the last game came out some six years ago at this point. The HBO adaptation is obviously a big part of that, but the fascination with the games remains, and there are millions out there who are desperate to see a third game come along at some point.

Naughty Dog is very much focused on Intergalactic: The Heretic Prophet for the foreseeable future, with that game not expected to drop until at least the back end of next year, if not 2028. That said, they haven't forgotten about the stealth series that elevated the studio to a whole new level, and the developer continues to maintain both games with updates and fresh releases.

Now, the latest of those is now live, and it includes a very fun set of cosmetics that could make your next run considerably more stylish than usual.

Over on Twitter/X, the official page for The Last of Us shared a post confirming the release of two outfits for The Last of Us Part II Remastered, available to those who pass a certain milestone. The post reads as follows:

"Players who complete The Last of Us Part II Remastered's Chronological mode can unlock two No Return skins inspired by Uncharted: a Nathan Drake outfit for Joel and a Sam Drake outfit for Tommy. Available via Patch v2.1.0 (PS5) and Patch v1.5 (PC)."

As the name suggests, the Chronological Mode is an official free game mode (released via Patch 2.1.0 on PS5 and Patch 1.5 on PC) that restructures the game's non-linear narrative into a strict, step-by-step linear order. That means you'll be pretty much constantly flashing back between the events of Ellie and Abby, which actually does provide a much clearer avenue to understanding what happened and when.

While it isn't the superior way to play the game, it is an interesting alternative for those who have already played through the game as intended. It also further underlines just how close they come to each other at times, and gives players a new appreciation of just how high the stakes are for both.

You will need both an active internet connection and a PSN account (if you're on console) to get the outfits downloaded, though they should automatically appear in your collection options once you have met the requirements.

As for the series itself, it's a safe bet that The Last of Us won't be entirely done for a long time to come yet. Though Naughty Dog and Neil Druckmann haven't gone as far as giving us confirmation that The Last of Us III is in production, they have previously confirmed that they are actively working on other projects in the same universe. Those could end up being spin-offs, but as long as the games tell similar stories, it's difficult to see a world where fans would care too much, given how patient they have been so far.

The Last of Us is a post-apocalyptic action-adventure franchise created by Neil Druckmann and Bruce Straley, developed by Naughty Dog. It centers around survivors in a world ravaged by a fungal infection turning humans into aggressive creatures. The story primarily follows Joel and Ellie in the original game, while the sequel adds new characters like Abby. The franchise includes video games, a television series, comics, and other media.

The Great Outdoors quest is one of the first you'll find after leaving your initial area in WoW: Forever, in which you meet an NPC who teaches you about camping.

It's an extremely simple one—you perch your backside in front of a campfire and then wait for a short while as a buff accumulates, teaching you the importance of regularly resting at fires for their passive bonus. Basically, you just:

Simple stuff, but this quest is bugged for some players. When they try to sit, it doesn't register, meaning you can't complete the first stage. I assume Blizzard will be working on a hotfix, but one solution that works for some players is pressing X to sit in front of the fire, then typing /sit into chat. Give it a try and see.

Otherwise, you might have to return to this one later, which is definitely frustrating, as this NPC's follow-up quests introduce you to different aspects of some early-game professions and crafting.

Discord is ditching Electron and its new app uses less RAM

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Discord is moving its apps fully over to the Rust programming language.

Discord is moving its apps fully over to the Rust programming language.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Discord is finally giving up on Electron, the cross-platform framework that lets developers build desktop apps using web technologies. Electron web apps are notoriously heavy on system resources, and Discord is now moving its desktop app fully to Rust — a modern programming language designed for speed and memory safety.

“We’ve been early adopters of Rust since 2018 and a lot of our infra, voice tech, and even some of the current client is already Rust, the holy grail was getting the whole client there,” explains Discord chief technology officer Stanislav Vishnevskiy in a post on X. “That means Electron goes away, and so does React Native, mobile keeps native UI but shares app code with desktop via Rust.”

Vishnevskiy also shared an image of the Discord Windows client using just 290MB of RAM, which is a clear improvement as you only have to participate in a few servers and RAM usage can easily hit 1GB or more using the current Windows app.

It’s not clear when Discord will make the full switch over to Rust, but Vishnevskiy says the company “hope to get it into testing with users soon.”

Max, il calcio semplice è finito: la rivincita di Farioli. Anche contro chi non l’ha scelto

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Il calcio è semplice, sostiene da sempre Massimiliano Allegri. Poi arriva Francesco Farioli, gli rifila quattro gol e dimostra che la semplicità, di questi tempi, è il risultato di un lavoro molto più complesso. Il 4-1 del Porto al Napoli non è una sentenza definitiva su due allenatori, ma racconta parecchio delle differenze tra due modi di intendere il calcio. Allegri continua a rivendicare la centralità dei giocatori, dell'esperienza e della gestione dei momenti. Farioli studia, prepara, organizza e interviene. Vince. Al Dragao ha trovato la prima vittoria in questa Champions League dopo tre sconfitte consecutive, sfruttando anche le difficoltà di un Napoli decimato dagli infortuni. Ma le assenze non contano più dell'attenzione ai dettagli.

Farioli non deve più dimostrare di essere un allenatore interessante. Ha 37 anni, ha lavorato in Turchia, Francia e Olanda, ha perso uno scudetto con l'Ajax in maniera clamorosa e si è rialzato conquistando il campionato portoghese con il Porto. In questa stagione è ripartito con dieci vittorie nelle prime dieci giornate di Liga Portugal. Nel frattempo, in Italia, le grandi società continuano a rivolgersi prevalentemente agli allenatori che conoscono già, spesso appartenenti alla stessa generazione. Per carità, non è obbligatorio scegliere un tecnico giovane per essere moderni. Ma l'esperienza non può diventare l'unico criterio di selezione. Farioli ha costruito all'estero un curriculum che molti suoi colleghi, considerati più affidabili soltanto perché hanno allenato in Serie A, possono invidiargli.

C'è anche un risvolto particolarmente significativo. Aurelio De Laurentiis apprezza Farioli da anni, lo ha seguito e valutato. Quando ha dovuto scegliere il successore di Antonio Conte, però, il Napoli ha puntato su Allegri, preferito anche a Vincenzo Italiano. Ieri sera, il presidente azzurro ha visto cosa avrebbe potuto portare a Napoli quell'allenatore che conosceva così bene e che non ha mai scelto. Alla vigilia Farioli aveva scherzato sull'interesse del club partenopeo, definendolo ormai andato in prescrizione. Il campo gli ha regalato una soddisfazione decisamente più concreta. Il problema, per Allegri e per il calcio italiano, non è riconoscere che Farioli abbia vinto una partita. È accettare che esistano allenatori capaci di ottenere risultati attraverso percorsi diversi, senza avere alle spalle vent'anni di panchine nella nostra Serie A. Max ha conquistato sei scudetti e raggiunto due finali di Champions League: nessuna sconfitta cancellerà quei risultati. Ma il passato non può essere una garanzia per il presente, né un argomento per liquidare chi propone qualcosa di diverso. Farioli, ormai, è più di una semplice proposta.

Critical security vulnerability affects OpenSSH on Alpine Linux systems

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Version 10.6p2 of OpenSSH has been released with a workaround for a critical vulnerability that affects Linux systems that use musl libc with PAM disabled, such as Alpine Linux. OpenSSH developer Damien Miller said:

If you operate a Linux system that uses musl libc without PAM (Alpine Linux is one example) then stop reading and set PasswordAuthentication=no or disable SSH for these systems IMMEDIATELY.

This vulnerability does not exist on non-Linux systems, Linux/glibc systems and Linux systems that use PAM for password authentication.

We consider the root cause of this bug to be outside OpenSSH itself, but we're in a good position to deliver a mitigation quickly.

The Alpine Linux project has announced that it will be backporting the OpenSSH fix "all the way back to Alpine 3.16", which was released in May 2022.

This is an absolutely massive day for PC Gamer writers called Joshua Wolens.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Reader, a confession: each night, before I retire to my chambers, I light a small votive candle. Bowing over it, I clasp my hands and beseech the ghost of Josh Sawyer (who is not dead) to return to the Pillars of Eternity games and add the weirdly-decent gamepad support they got on console to their PC iterations. I am an old man who wants to play them on my Steam Deck, and the existing Steam Input templates don't cut it for me.

My prayers are answered. In an announcement yesterday, Obsidian declared that now is the time of the Pillars of Eternity and Deadfire: Controller Support Public Test. Both PoE 1 and 2 have ongoing Steam betas that will allow you to play with full, start-to-finish controller support. You can opt into them and try the new feature for yourself by visiting each game's properties and opting into their respective November 4 beta versions.

Being betas, they're a touch buggy right now. Indeed, one of Obsidian's very first notices to players in the public test's announcement is "This is a public test, so please expect bugs", but I'm nevertheless impressed by the limited time I've spent mucking around with the games on Steam Deck.

There are a couple of misaligned gamepad prompts, and a very slight amount of awkwardness with the analogue movement, but frankly my main takeaway is 'Dang, it's kind of wild how well these isometric CRPGs adjust to non-keyboard-and-mouse support'. Which, yeah, is how I felt about the console versions too.

The public test runs until November 25, and the devs will use the data gathered to iron out wrinkles like those I just listed for a full release.

It's not all about gamepad support, by the way. Just, you know, mostly. Obsidian also announced patch 1.3.10.0 for PoE 1 and patch 5.1.0.0 for Deadfire. In PoE 1's case, the patch mostly smooths out some minor niggling bugs with the UI and the game's recently implemented turn-based mode.

Deadfire's patch is meatier: that game's older turn-based mode has been rejigged to integrate the improvements from PoE 1's turn-based mode. Characters no longer take a single turn per round (instead, they move as much as their initiative allows), lethality has been greatly increased—that kinda thing.

I'm a die-hard real-time-with-pause guy, so it's all fairly academic to me, but PoE 1's turn-based innovations got a lot of plaudits from players who were very hopeful they would end up ported back to Deadfire. My friends, this one is for you.

He doesn't refer to Cyberpunk by name, but it's quite clear which RPG he's referring to

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

GTA 6 is officially two weeks away – to the day, at the time of writing – and after its multiple delays, it's felt like an extremely long time coming. Take-Two CEO Strauss Zelnick clearly stands by those decisions, though, as he seemingly implies that Cyberpunk 2077's disastrous launch is an example of why knowing when to run is a strength, not a weakness.

In a new interview with the Wall Street Journal (below), the boss of the Rockstar Games owner is asked about the game's delays and how they unfolded. While Zelnick freely admits that "these are very difficult decisions to make," he argues that when "your mission is to make the best entertainment on earth, and part of your three-part strategy is to be the most creative, sometimes you have to make these expensive and inconvenient decisions in service of that mission and goals."

Zelnick elaborates: "There are a lot of companies who have a great mission statement, a stated strategy, and a culture, but then when push comes to shove, you know, they do something that's at odds with that."

He clarifies that doesn't mean he'll "just say yes" any time someone asks him for something – in fact, he refers to another situation at Take-Two where he conversely said, "actually, we're shipping." Zelnick quickly adds: "Oh, by the way, thankfully I was right. Imagine if I'd been wrong."

However, he continues, folks often forget, "what happens when people take the other approach? The other approach being, 'we're slavishly devoted to a schedule, and we're just going to ship despite what is going on.'

"I'm not going to throw any competitors under the bus, but if you know anything about the video game business, in the past few years, there was a very high-profile release that fell into this category, and they shipped, and they had to pull the title after shipping."

While he doesn't name it, it sounds like Zelnick is referring to Cyberpunk 2077. Barely a week after its notoriously buggy launch, which was especially disappointing after years of hype, CD Projekt Red opted to temporarily pull it from the PlayStation Store and offered refunds to folks who'd already spent their money on it.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

Obviously, The Witcher studio eventually managed to turn things around for Cyberpunk, and many updates later, it's been very successful and is widely celebrated as a fantastic RPG, but the shadow of its extremely rocky release is ultimately always going to loom over it to some extent.

13 full years since the launch of GTA 5, neither Rockstar Games nor its owner, Take-Two, would have wanted a similar outcome for GTA 6 – a game that's got expectations so high I'm personally wondering if it'll actually be able to meet them for fans, even if it ends up being a 10/10 release. But holding back the release until everyone is confident that it's actually ready is a good start and will hopefully leave Rockstar in the best position it can be come November 19.

Thailand to block Bitget in December over unlicensed crypto trading

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Thailand’s SEC filed a criminal complaint against Bitget’s operator, with access to its website and app set to be blocked from Dec. 5.

Thailand’s securities regulator has filed a criminal complaint against BG Limited, the operator of cryptocurrency exchange Bitget, for allegedly operating without a license.

Thailand’s Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) said Thursday that it had notified the Ministry of Digital Economy and Society to block access to Bitget from Dec. 5. The restriction will cover Bitget’s website, other associated domains and its mobile app.

The regulator advised users to take action regarding assets held on the platform before the deadline.

According to the SEC, Bitget offered crypto trading services between 2022 and 2025, charged trading fees and promoted its services through Thai-language websites and social media channels. The regulator said these activities violated Thailand’s digital asset business licensing requirements.

The regulator filed the complaint with Thailand’s Economic Crime Suppression Division for investigation. Prosecutors will decide whether to bring charges, while courts will determine criminal liability.

Thailand’s Ministry of Digital Economy and Society has authority to restrict access to unlicensed crypto trading platforms under a 2025 decree aimed at protecting investors and combating financial crime.

Bitget did not immediately respond to Cointelegraph’s request for comment.

Binance Revokes API Keys, Security Issue Found at SignalPlus

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Binance has revoked all API keys associated with SignalPlus after the digital asset trading infrastructure provider identified a potential security vulnerability, prompting the world's largest cryptocurrency exchange to take measures to protect users.

The exchange confirmed Thursday that the decision was intended to prevent possible unauthorized access through connections established between Binance accounts and SignalPlus. Binance emphasized that its internal systems had not been compromised and that customer funds remained safe.

The precise nature of the security issue remains unclear. Neither the extent of the potential vulnerability nor the number of users affected has been publicly established. There has also been no confirmation that attackers accessed customer accounts or that the incident resulted in financial losses.

Following a potential security issue reported by third-party platform SignalPlus, we have proactively removed all API keys linked to SignalPlus as an immediate precautionary measure. Binance's own systems are unaffected. User funds remain safe. If you used SignalPlus, please log into your Binance account directly to review your positions and open orders. We recommend waiting until SignalPlus confirms full resolution before rebinding any API keys, and always enabling IP whitelisting. We also encourage all third-party platforms integrated with Binance to review their security controls, monitor for unusual activity and contact us immediately if they detect any concern

The development underscores the security challenges associated with third-party infrastructure in cryptocurrency markets, where trading platforms increasingly depend on external applications for execution, analytics and automation.

API-related vulnerabilities have previously created risks for exchange users. In 2022, the exposure of API credentials associated with trading platform 3Commas triggered security warnings and key revocations across multiple cryptocurrency exchanges.

Binance's security documentation advises users to revoke potentially compromised credentials immediately and restrict API permissions to necessary functions.

For affected SignalPlus users, restoring automated trading functionality may require generating new credentials once the integration is deemed secure.

SignalPlus provides trading infrastructure and analytical tools for cryptocurrency derivatives markets, allowing traders to manage positions and execute strategies across exchanges through API integrations.

These connections enable third-party applications to interact with exchange accounts without requiring users to transfer their assets off-platform. However, depending on the permissions granted, compromised API credentials can potentially expose accounts to unauthorized trading activity.

Binance has effectively severed existing integrations between its platform and SignalPlus. The measure could potentially disrupt automated trading strategies that rely on those connections.

Trimble Integrates Drone Data Processing Directly into WorksManager Software

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

WorksManager Drone software turns flight data into actionable site data â all from one integrated and connected platform

WESTMINSTER, Colo., Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Trimble today announced TrimbleÂ® WorksManager Drone processing software, enabling civil contractors to process drone data in the cloud, directly within the Trimble ecosystem.

This new photogrammetry functionality makes it easy for contractors to process and overlay true-to-scale flight data with project designs, machine data, as-builts and other site data to calculate volumes, take measurements and verify material for monitoring site progress.

Trimble WorksManager is a cloud-based software and data management tool that connects the construction jobsite to the office, making it easy to share project files and design updates across the entire team. With the addition of Trimble WorksManager Drone functionality, site supervisors and project managers can now quickly and easily access drone data showing the current state of the jobsite, and view it together with the latest design file and machine data.

"Civil contractors shouldn't have to juggle fragmented tools in order to see what's happening on-site," said Elwyn McLachlan, vice president, civil construction product and strategy, Trimble. "WorksManager Drone closes the loop between photogrammetry insights and ground action giving teams a complete and accurate picture of their jobsite. This connectivity further enhances the depth and breadth of the Trimble ecosystem to power each part of the design, build and operate lifecycle of a construction project."

"In the past, verifying on-site material volumes required a lot of manual coordination," said Joe Zapien, field technology manager at Markham Contracting. "Trimble WorksManager Drone allows us to quickly process flight data, putting 3D visuals and cut-fill site metrics right in the hands of our foremen and project managers. Bringing that real-time visibility to the field gives us the clarity to make immediate, informed design changes and site decisions."

WorksManager Drone is another example of how Trimble is bringing highly accurate site data into the Trimble Connect unified construction platform to drive meaningful operational improvements across the design-build-operate lifecycle. Trimble's platform-first approach is a further step forward in breaking down data silos to drive greater efficiency, improve margins and meet tight timelines.

AvailabilityTrimble WorksManager Drone software is expected to be available in January 2027 through Trimble's global dealer channel for use with DJI and Wingtra drones. Cristiano Ronaldo has called time on his international career with Portugal after 234 caps

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Cristiano Ronaldo is bowing out from the international game after a staggering 234 appearances for the Portuguese national team.

The 41-year-old Al-Nassr forward was an unused substitute as Portugal beat Norway in the Nations League back in September and subsequently chose to withdraw from the team's camp for the upcoming October fixtures.

Ahead of November's squad announcement, Ronaldo has released a five-minute video message, mainly aimed towards Portuguese football supporters, announcing his decision to retire from the international game. Here is every word.

“To the Portuguese people, The day has come to hand back the shirt and the captain's armband of the national team that belongs to all of us. I never felt that it was exclusively mine, but for 23 years, I had the privilege and responsibility of wearing it, defending it, and carrying it with me to stadiums all around the world.

"Today, the moment has come to pass it on to the future. It was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made with the head alone. They are also made with the heart. I am bringing to an end a journey that began in August 2003 and changed my life forever. When I look back and think about everything I experienced with the national team, I don't just see matches, goals, records, or trophies. I see people. I see immigrants who, for 90 minutes, felt a little closer to Portugal. I see millions of Portuguese people whom I never met personally, but who became part of my life. Because, at the end of the day, football is much bigger than a game. Football is about people.

"Throughout my entire career with the national team, I always tried to push beyond my limits. I wanted to win, I wanted to be the best, I wanted to make history. I was always demanding of myself. I always wanted more. I always believed we could go further, and I gave everything I had for this shirt.

"There were perfect afternoons and nights. There were victories I will never forget. There were defeats that still hurt to remember today. I also failed. I also suffered. There were moments when I couldn't give the Portuguese people what they wanted so badly. But I never gave up. And above all, I never stopped feeling proud to represent Portugal.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

"Over these years, I learned some very simple things: goals come and go, trophies end up in museums, records are eventually broken, but there is something that time can never erase. The place I hold in people's hearts.

"If I ever made you happy, if one of my goals ever made you hug someone, forget a worry, or feel proud of our country, then it was all worth it. Because being Portuguese is also about that. We suffer together. We get back up together. We believe again.

"Portugal will always need young people who dream, players who never give up, and new generations who believe it is possible to reach places no one has reached before. Because a country that continues to create new heroes is a country that never stops believing in itself.

"To my national team teammates, I want to leave a special message. Thank you for every run, every hug, every argument, every moment of silence before stepping onto the pitch, every victory we celebrated together, and every defeat that made us stronger. I had the privilege of being your captain, but no captain walks alone, and you are all part of my story. To all the coaches, directors, doctors, physiotherapists, kit managers, staff members, and the many people who worked with me throughout these years, thank you.

"Many of you never appeared in the photographs, but without you, none of those photographs would have been possible. To my family, thank you for sharing this dream with me. Thank you for understanding the absences, the sacrifices, and the moments when the national team had to take up a space that also belonged to you. If I made it this far, it was because we always walked hand in hand.

"And to the Portuguese people, to every Portuguese person around the world, I want to tell you one last thing. There is an image that I will always carry with me. A moment when the national anthem begins. I look around me. I see my teammates. I see the stands. I see children, mothers, fathers, and grandparents. I see Portuguese people who travelled from so far away just to be there. And during those moments, we stop being different. There are no clubs. There are no colours. There are no classes. There are no professions. There are no opinions. There is only one passion: Portugal."

Joe joined FourFourTwo as senior digital writer in July 2025 after five years covering Leeds United in the Championship and Premier League. Joe's 'Mastermind' specialist subject is 2000s-era Newcastle United having had a season ticket at St. James' Park for 10 years before relocating to Leeds and later London. Joe takes a keen interest in youth football, covering PL2, U21 Euros, as well as U20 and U17 World Cups in the past, in addition to hosting the industry-leading football recruitment-focused SCOUTED podcast. He is also one of the lucky few to have 'hit top bins' as a contestant on Soccer AM. It wasn't a shin-roller.

New findings suggest that "food challenges" can help prevent children from being saddled with unnecessary allergy diagnoses.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A peanut allergy diagnosis in infancy can mean years of avoiding the food or undergoing treatments to build tolerance. But a recent study suggests that more than one-third of babies thought to have the allergy may stop reacting badly to peanuts within months of that diagnosis.

In an analysis published in The Journal of Allergy and Clinical Immunology: In Practice, researchers conducted supervised "food challenges" with infants diagnosed with a peanut allergy. These challenges involved giving the babies increasing amounts of peanut protein, with the intention of confirming whether they indeed had an allergy.

The team found that more than 1 in 3 infants who underwent the food challenge tolerated the peanut protein, meaning they didn't experience symptoms of an allergic reaction.

"This rate is much higher than we thought," study co-author Dr. Timothy Brettig, a pediatric allergist at the National Allergy Centre of Excellence in Australia, told Live Science.

Between April 2024 and October 2025, Brettig and colleagues retroactively reviewed the medical records of 1,275 infants who had been referred to Australian public hospitals after having a reaction to peanuts before their first birthday. These babies had first been assessed by allergy specialists at a median of 10 months of age.

For their initial diagnosis, the babies had to have a history of an allergic reaction to peanuts and show evidence of peanut sensitization on an allergy test. These tests included a skin-prick test, in which peanut extract is placed on the skin before it's lightly pricked, to check for a local reaction; and blood tests that detect antibodies against peanut proteins.

"Children under 1 year of age are an interesting group, as their immune system is still in rapid development and existing allergy tests may not reflect their allergic status well," said Dr. Alexandra Santos, a professor of pediatric allergy at King's College London who was not involved in the study. While these tests can hint that a child is sensitized to certain foods, they don't necessarily confirm that a baby will have an allergic reaction upon eating said food; the tests can also give false positives.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

Of the 1,275 infants, 778 later underwent a food challenge roughly six months after their first recorded allergic reaction; at that point, their median age was 13 months old. They were given up to seven gradually increasing doses of peanut protein, ranging from 15 to 1,300 milligrams. These doses were given at 15-minute intervals while medical staff monitored the children for signs of an allergic reaction.

Nearly 35% of the participants — 271 kids — completed the challenge without reacting.

"This is an excellent article," said Dr. Drew Bird, a pediatric allergist at the University of Texas Southwestern Medical Center who was not involved in the study. "The take-home message is to strongly consider a supervised oral food challenge when an infant's peanut allergy diagnosis is uncertain."

Bird emphasized that these challenges should ideally be done before a child starts oral immunotherapy, a treatment in which patients regularly consume carefully measured amounts of the food they are allergic to. The dose is gradually increased under medical supervision, with the goal of helping the patient tolerate greater amounts of the allergen and reducing the risk of reactions from accidental exposure.

An incorrect diagnosis can prompt families to unnecessarily avoid exposing their children to peanuts.

The starting dose is often determined through a similar oral food challenge to the one used in this study, though. So in theory, that challenge would help reveal whether the child needs treatment in the first place.

Bird cautioned that food challenges should be performed by a board-certified allergist-immunologist and an experienced allergy team in a setting equipped to treat anaphylaxis — a severe, potentially life-threatening allergic reaction.

In this study, 507 infants reacted during the food challenge, and of those, 61 developed anaphylaxis and were treated with epinephrine (the hormone in EpiPens).

"In an ideal world, with infinite resources," Santos said, "all patients except for those with a history of severe reactions would have a food challenge for diagnosis."

Because that is not the case, the families of infants whose allergy diagnosis is uncertain may be advised to avoid peanut products while they await a formal food challenge, she noted. But in the interim, they may miss an opportunity to help the child develop tolerance and, in turn, increase the risk that their allergy will persist.

Food challenges should be prioritized for infants whose allergy diagnoses are uncertain, she said. This would include babies who have low levels of peanut-specific antibodies in their blood or who show only small welts on skin-prick tests.

This article is for informational purposes only and is not meant to offer medical advice.

Brettig, T. W., McDonald, E., West, D., et al. (2026). An oral food challenge is required for accurate diagnosis of peanut allergy in infants prior to oral immunotherapy: Evaluation of the Allergy Development to an Accelerated Pathway to Tolerance Peanut Oral Immunotherapy Program. The Journal of Allergy and Clinical Immunology: In Practice. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jaip.2026.08.044

He regularly writes for The Hindu and has contributed to The Wire Science, where he conveys complex biomedical research to the public in accessible language. Beyond science writing, he enjoys creating and reading fiction that blends myth, memory, and melancholy into surreal tales exploring grief, identity, and the quiet magic of self-discovery. In his free time, he loves long walks with his dog and motorcycling across The Himalayas.

SpaceX's plan to build more data center compute than Google's entire lifetime capacity in just 1.5 years is possible, say analysts

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elon Musk's venture does appear to be moving much faster than its competitors.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A scant two weeks ago, Elon Musk made yet another bold claim: he was "highly confident" that SpaceX could build 10 GW of data center capacity in 2027 alone. The grandiose statement raised a few eyebrows, but in a detailed write-up, the analysts at Semiaccurate explain how that's actually doable... if the stars align. That +10 GW figure over the existing 3 GW would mean the company had built in as much data center capacity in 1.5 years as Google did in its entire existence, according to the post. The site notes that should this forecast ring true, that would make SpaceX the fastest-building hyperscaler on the planet, far outpacing every other player.

As proof of progress, the analysts point to how quickly the Colossus 1 and Colossus 2 buildouts are progressing, estimated from aerial shots and available information. They note that Colossus 2's power generation capacity went from only 0.4 GW in January to a projected 2.4 GW by the time the year is out, at the rate that the relevant hardware is popping up. The site tallies up 9 GW of potential data center capacity across the sites it's tracking, including Colossus and Starbase facilities.

Notably, the facility includes 89 turbines capable of delivering approximately 2.5 GW, helped along by a total of 1377 Tesla battery Megapacks, all already on the campus. Due to emissions regulations, the facility will only eventually be allowed to operate roughly 1.24 GW of its own turbines.

Even with the 1.2 GW eventually coming in from the grid, there's a 300 MW shortfall to be filled by the batteries, which Semianalysis expects will top up with compute load variation and possibly external power. SpaceX has an agreement with the state of Mississippi that lets it temporarily operate the extra turbines, with a promised phase-out by July 2027.

As of September 25, Elon Musk had stated that Colossus 2 already had 550,000 GPUs working, presumably in the extant Macrohard and Macroharder facility. At the time, SpaceX was reportedly gunning to have its Gun Range and Project SPAM centers ready in short order. Judging by the photos, they appear to be progressing at a rapid pace, as there are visible facilities where there was nothing but loose dirt a few months ago — though nothing says the racks of GPUs are in there yet.

Semianalysis' team remarks that SpaceX's builds have become so fast that they kick off well before it decides how the facility will be powered, and the respective power supplies come in. We've all seen the news about the scarcity of GPUs and RAM, but for a data center, just having enough power is problem enough.

If SpaceX can keep its stream of gas turbines coming along, that's quite the achievement by itself. The analysts think that Tesla may be manufacturing mechanical and electrical equipment in modules, presumably ready to install directly from the factory, or even trucks. Heck, the aforementioned temporary turbines are mounted on truck flatbeds as "mobile" power generation, seemingly in a bid to skirt regulations.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

In the hyperscaler world, speed is the name of the game, particularly in these trying times when there are mounting suspicions that not all players will even make it to the finish line. Compute capacity being built and eventually becoming available is enticing for AI players, yes, but they're willing to pay through the nose for anything they can get their hands on right now, as current demand far outstrips supply.

Having facilities built doesn't mean that they're actually working, however, or that they're functioning at their expected rate. Zoning, permits, grid connections, power generation constraints, water supply, and public opposition are some of the few problems that have plagued builds most everywhere, none of which relate to the existence and installation of computing hardware.

Microsoft's Satya Nadella put it clearly: "You may actually have a bunch of chips sitting in inventory that I can’t plug in. In fact, that is my problem today." That's far from the only report that a significant percentage of AI accelerators are still stuck in warehouses, on hold until they can go fulfill their duties in a server rack somewhere.

Since building a datacenter involves many moving parts, even assuming that Colossus 2 wraps up as quickly as it appears to be, that doesn't necessarily mean that SpaceX will be able to replicate its success across its other sites. There is no luck involved in engineering, yes, but some factors like availability of hardware and materials, changes in legal winds, and public opposition are hard to predict, even for someone with resources as extensive as Elon Musk.

And yet, the folks at Semianalysis note that two data centers at Starbase are undergoing rapid construction, suggesting that SpaceX can indeed replicate Colossus' success. They attribute the speed to SpaceX having most of the infrastructure and power generation hardware effectively in-house, via Tesla's manufacturing ability. If that assessment holds, the firm could be bypassing supplier lead times that other hyperscalers have to contend with.

Even if SpaceX doesn't reach its "10 GW in a year" target, if the Colossus buildouts are anything to go by, the company may well be moving faster than anyone else in this space. Metrics aside, in the AI gold rush, coming first is all that's needed to win.
