The ‘Ocarina of Time’ Remake Is Finally Here, but Why Does Link Look so Yassified?

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After 28 years, the gamer’s game is getting a big remake.

A fully redone version of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time arrives Thursday exclusively on the Switch 2. Nintendo has been releasing trailers and screenshots in the run-up to the launch, and the imagery, while whetting appetites, has also left fans with an overwhelming question: Why does Link look like that?

Specifically, child Link. Ocarina of Time allows players to time-hop and explore levels as child and adult versions of the game’s main protagonist. Gameplay starts as child Link in the Kokiri Forest, which looks more lush and verdant than ever in the remake. But aesthetic upgrades have also been applied to child Link, resulting in a curled-eyelash, laminated-eyebrow, douyin-lipstick yassified elf boy.

Fans have been quick to compare the design to the human-vampire hybrid Renesmee from Twilight and the Gerber Baby. “He looks so microwaveable,” one Reddit user wrote.

The changes feel all the more stark when juxtaposed with the remake’s background nonplayer characters, who remain noticeably simpler and more reminiscent of the original game’s character designs.

The original Ocarina was limited to minimal polygonal shapes and textures because of the small storage capacity in N64 cartridges, and these technical limitations produced what became a recognizable and beloved visual style. Thanks to the Switch 2’s larger storage capacity and higher memory bus speed, designers have been able to add details to Link and the other main characters that wouldn’t have been possible in the original.

Beyond better technology, though, people have different expectations of video games from major publishers. Interconnecting level designs, intricate storylines, and highly realistic environment and character model details are table stakes for contemporary AAA games. Video games are also more mainstream, and once-regional media like anime and manga have become global phenomena over the past three decades, influencing taste in the larger culture.

Clayton Lundgren, a former quality assurance tester for the game developer Naughty Dog who currently streams Ocarina of Time along with other games on Twitch, sees nothing accidental about Link’s new look. “For a decision as important as the key art for the main character, this is the kind of thing that is gonna go past a lot of quality inspection higher up than the person that drew it,” he says. “This is 20-plus years of merchandising that you’re committing to.”

Since The Legend of Zelda launched the franchise in 1986, Link has existed in many forms. Though his long hat, pointy ears, blond hair, green tunic, and Master Sword are staples of his design, none of these characteristics have been stable across his 40 years. He’s had pink hair, his tunic has been blue and red. He’s even been a tree.

The remake takes the evolution a step further, though. Lundgren, who has at least 1,000 hours in Ocarina across speed runs, challenge runs, and community competitions, immediately realized that this week’s release is a ground-up remake when he watched the game’s new trailer. For many fans, the rich detail of the underbrush and sunbeams in Kokiri Forest visible in the teaser video were proof this isn’t the same Ocarina. But Lundgren saw something else: The undulation in the ground was different.

A 2023 version of Link from Tears of the Kingdom.

Unlike the 2011 Zelda remake for the 3DS, which uses the same underlying geometries as the original, the 2026 remake is a complete overhaul, from the superficial textures to the bones underneath. And if everything is different, how is this the same Link we’ve played and loved before?

Luke Cuddy, a philosophy professor at Southwestern College who has edited books about The Legend of Zelda, offers the “Ship of Theseus” thought experiment as a way to think through the conundrum. The famous philosophical riddle asks whether the true ship is the one Theseus is currently sailing or the original constituent parts that have been replaced over time. If one thinks the true ship is the one Theseus is currently sailing, they believe there is something beyond the physical body that makes something what it is.

“There are only a few minimal features that are continuous over time with Link,” says Cuddy. Aside from a few visual and narrative touchstones—and the occasional “Hyah!” or “Hyup!” voice line—there isn’t much else that Link is. “For me, that allows for a lot of malleability.”

That malleability is cushioned by other forms of continuity. Beyond aesthetic similarities and a shared title across Zelda games, contemporary games that were influenced by the original Ocarina are now shaping the remake in turn, and norms of play set by speedrunners and challenge runners of the original continue to shape engagement across gaming communities.

But another force is fueling the current scrutiny and anxiety over the remake, one that haunts every contemporary artistic endeavor: Was generative AI involved?

Artificial intelligence has long been part of video games designed to respond in real time to players’ actions. But these days, when the topic of AI use in video games comes up, it’s usually in reference to AI tools being used to make the visual parts of a game, the storyboards, the written scripts, or the spoken dialog.

The backlash to these tools is growing, rooted in concerns about the environment, artists’ intellectual property, and aesthetic taste. Players are increasingly worried that they’re engaging with media that betrays their values, and for a game like Ocarina, the possible juxtaposition of the original’s groundbreaking design with derivative and cannibalistic tools feels like a long way to fall.

Cuddy compares the “super-enhanced version of Link” in the remake to the output of an AI filter, and other fans are questioning why the designs look like they were run through DLSS 5, an animation upscaling tool that uses generative AI. Nintendo has confirmed its use of AMD FSR, a competing upscaling tool, though when WIRED asked if generative AI tools were also used in the character design process for the new game, the company declined to comment.

When one of the most beloved entries in a beloved game franchise gets a new coat of paint, fans of the game are of course going to scrutinize every aspect of the remake’s design. But Lundgren predicts that, based on Zelda fans’ history of embracing Link’s many incarnations in the past, “in two years we’ll think it looks great.”

STAT+: A closer look at ProMis Neurosciences and its Alzheimer’s drug

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Test your knowledge with our new weekday mini crossword

Adam Feuerstein is a senior writer and biotech columnist, reporting on the crossroads of drug development, business, Wall Street, and biotechnology. He is also a co-host of the weekly biotech podcast The Readout Loud and author of the newsletter Adam’s Biotech Scorecard. You can reach Adam on Signal at stataf.54.

Shares of ProMis Neurosciences, developer of an amyloid-targeting treatment for Alzheimer’s disease, sold off sharply last week in reaction to an announcement from Acumen Pharmaceuticals that its own Alzheimer’s drug, with a similar mechanism, failed to slow the cognitive or functional decline of patients with Alzheimer’s disease compared to a placebo in a mid-stage study.

“This was a complete overreaction in my opinion and not justified,” ProMis CEO Neil Warma told me, when I asked if investors were correct to assume the failure of Acumen’s drug presaged a similar fate for ProMis’ drug.

Adam Feuerstein is a senior writer and biotech columnist, reporting on the crossroads of drug development, business, Wall Street, and biotechnology. He is also a co-host of the weekly biotech podcast The Readout Loud and author of the newsletter Adam’s Biotech Scorecard. You can reach Adam on Signal at stataf.54.

BCE reports third quarter 2026 results

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This news release contains forward-looking statements. For a description of the related risk factors and assumptions, please see the section entitled "Caution Regarding Forward-Looking Statements" later in this news release. The information contained in this news release is unaudited.

MONTREAL, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- BCE Inc. (TSX: BCE) (NYSE: BCE) today reported results for the third quarter (Q3) of 2026.

"In the year since our Investor Day 2025, Bell's Q3 results show responsible investment in key growth areas and steadfast execution of our long-term priorities," said Mirko Bibic, President and CEO, BCE and Bell Canada.

Bell Business Markets remains a strong performer with revenue up 9.2%, including 41% growth in AI-powered solutions revenue. We also continued to advance Bell AI Fabric, including a non-binding MOU with the Government of Saskatchewan to explore up to 900 megawatts of additional capacity at our facility outside Regina, creating a potential path to 1.2 gigawatts of total capacity.

We also announced an agreement with La Caisse to sell a 49.9% stake in our newly formed wireless tower infrastructure company, allowing us to surface the value of our tower infrastructure assets while retaining control of the business.

Our commitment to 3.5x net debt leverage1 by the end of 2027 remains unchanged, and we remain focused on disciplined execution of our four strategic priorities. As we invest in the future to create value for shareholders, we are pleased with the progress we've made as we head into the end of 2026."

1 Adjusted EBITDA is a total of segments measure, adjusted net earnings and free cash flow are non-GAAP financial measures, adjusted EPS is a non-GAAP ratio, and net debt leverage ratio is a capital management measure. Refer to the Non-GAAP and Other Financial Measures section in this news release for more information on these measures.

2 Bell Business Markets (BBM) operating revenue includes wireline and wireless service and product revenue from large and medium retail business customers generated from the sale of communications services and AI-powered solutions. Communications revenue is comprised of: core connectivity services, including wireless, Internet, voice, and data network services sold directly to enterprise customers, as well as advanced cloud-based services such as Contact Center as a Service (CCaaS) and unified-communications-as-a-service (UCaaS). Al-powered solutions revenue is comprised of revenue from Ateko, Bell Cyber, and Bell Al Fabric.

Deliver the best fibre and wireless networks

Lead in enterprise with AI-powered solutions

Build a digital media and content powerhouse

5 Based on 5G/5G+ Network Performance Score, calculated by Rohde & Schwarz SwissQual AG, independent network analysts based in Switzerland, from data collected in Canada comparing data-only performance of other national wireless networks from March to June 2026. 5G+ results are defined as connectivity while using 3500MHz / 3800 MHz spectrum. Report available for download at bell.ca/5Gplus

($ millions except per share amounts) (unaudited)

Net earnings attributable to common shareholders

BCE operating revenues were $6,182 million in Q3 2026, up 2.2% compared to Q3 2025. This was the result of 1.6% higher service revenue of $5,412 million and a 6.9% increase in product revenue to $770 million.

Net earnings in Q3 decreased 84.6% to $701 million and net earnings attributable to common shareholders totalled $630 million, or $0.67 per share, down 86.0% and 86.2%, respectively.

Adjusted net earnings were down 5.2% in Q3 to $695 million, resulting in a 6.3% decrease in adjusted EPS to $0.74.

Adjusted EBITDA grew 0.9% in Q3 to $2,786 million, reflecting the contribution of Bell CTS U.S. and an 8.0% increase at Bell Media, partly offset by a 0.8% decrease at Bell CTS Canada.

BCE capital expenditures in Q3 were $1,330 million, up 49.3% from $891 million last year, corresponding to a capital intensity7 of 21.5%, compared to 14.7% in Q3 2025.

BCE cash flows from operating activities in Q3 were $2,218 million, up 15.9% from $1,914 million in Q3 2025.

Free cash flow was $854 million, down 14.9% from $1,003 million in Q3 2025, mainly due to higher capital expenditures, partly offset by higher cash flows from operating activities, excluding cash from income taxes paid on significant divestitures and acquisition and other costs paid.

6 Adjusted EBITDA margin is defined as adjusted EBITDA divided by operating revenues. Refer to the Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) section in this news release for more information on adjusted EBITDA margin.

7 Capital intensity is defined as capital expenditures divided by operating revenues. Refer to the Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) section in this news release for more information on capital intensity.

On August 1, 2025, BCE completed its acquisition of Ziply Fiber and created the Bell CTS U.S. segment. The results of BCE's Canadian wireless and wireline operations are reported under Bell CTS Canada.

Bell CTS operating revenues increased 1.3% to $5,476 million in Q3 2026 compared to Q3 2025, driven by both higher service and product revenue. The increase in service revenue reflects the contribution from Bell CTS U.S., which was included for only two months in Q3 2025, partly offset by a year-over-year decline at Bell CTS Canada.

Bell CTS adjusted EBITDA8 grew 0.2% in Q3 to $2,530 million, reflecting the contribution from Bell CTS U.S., partly offset by a year-over-year decline at Bell CTS Canada. Bell CTS adjusted EBITDA margin decreased 0.5 percentage points to 46.2% from 46.7% in Q3 2025. The margin decline reflected a higher proportion of lower-margin product sales and a 2.2% increase in operating costs, mainly due to a full quarter of Ziply Fiber's operating expenses compared to two months in Q3 2025.

Bell CTS retail residential NAS net losses3 increased by 5.8% to 48,659 in Q3 2026, reflecting increased net losses at Bell CTS Canada, partly offset by fewer net losses at Bell CTS U.S.

Bell CTS' retail residential NAS customer base3,11,12 totalled 1,586,229 at the end of Q3 2026, representing a 10.2% decline compared to Q3 2025. The decrease reflects declines at both Bell CTS Canada and Bell CTS U.S.

8 Bell CTS adjusted EBITDA is a total of segments measure. Refer to the Non-GAAP and Other Financial Measures section in this news release for more information on this measure.

Bell CTS Canada operating revenue decreased 0.2% to $5,238 million in Q3 2026 compared to Q3 2025, due to lower service revenue, partly offset by higher product revenue.

Bell CTS Canada service revenue was down 1.3% in Q3 to $4,468 million, reflecting:

Bell CTS Canada product revenue increased 6.9% in Q3 to $770 million, reflecting greater equipment sales to large business customers, including the timing of certain wireline equipment deals.

Bell CTS Canada adjusted EBITDA decreased 0.8% in Q3 to $2,435 million, reflecting the flow-through of lower year-over-year service revenue and 0.3% higher operating costs. Together with a greater proportion of lower-margin product sales, these factors contributed to a 0.3 percentage-point margin decline to 46.5% from 46.8% in Q3 2025. The increase in operating costs this quarter is attributed to:

These cost factors were partly offset by cost reduction initiatives and operating efficiencies.

Mobile phone blended ARPU14 was down 1.7% to $57.07 in Q3 2026 from $58.04 in Q3 2025, reflecting:

Mobile connected device3 net activations decreased 49.9% in Q3 2026, compared to the same period last year, mainly due to higher business Internet of Things (IoT) deactivations.

Bell CTS U.S. operating revenues were $238 million in Q3 2026, compared to $160 million in Q3 2025. The increase primarily reflects the inclusion of a full quarter of Ziply Fiber's results, compared to only two months following its acquisition on August 1, 2025. Growth in Internet revenue from the continued expansion of Ziply Fiber's FTTP footprint was offset by ongoing declines in legacy voice and data services.

Bell CTS U.S. adjusted EBITDA was $95 million in Q3 2026, compared to $71 million in Q3 2025. Operating costs were $143 million, compared to $89 million. The Q3 2025 comparatives include only two months of Ziply Fiber. Adjusted EBITDA margin was 39.9%, down 4.5 percentage points from 44.4%, reflecting acquisition-related changes to the cost base, higher payments to the Network FiberCo partnership and the flow-through of lower legacy voice and data revenue.

Advertising revenue was up 9.0% in Q3 2026, reflecting strong sports advertising performance driven by the FIFA World Cup 2026â¢ and growth in digital video advertising revenue17, partly offset by continued softness in traditional advertising demand.

Bell Media adjusted EBITDA was up 8.0% to $256 million in Q3 2026 on the flow-through of higher operating revenue. However, margin declined 0.5 percentage points to 31.9% from 32.4% in Q3 2025, reflecting a 10.5% increase in operating costs associated with the FIFA World Cup 2026â¢ and contractual rights increases for premium content, including to support the growth of Crave. These factors were partly offset by cost reduction initiatives.

17 Digital video advertising revenue is defined as advertising revenue from digital platforms including web sites, mobile apps and connected TV apps.

18 Digital revenues are comprised of advertising revenue from digital platforms including web sites, mobile apps, ad-supported subscription tiers on Crave, connected TV apps, and out-of-home (OOH) digital assets/platforms, as well as advertising procured through Bell digital buying platforms and subscription revenue from direct-to-consumer services and video-on-demand services.

BCE's Board of Directors has declared a quarterly dividend of $0.4375 per common share, payable on January 15, 2027 to shareholders of record at the close of business on December 15, 2026.

BCE uses various financial measures to assess its business performance. Certain of these measures are calculated in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards or GAAP while certain other measures do not have a standardized meaning under GAAP. We believe that our GAAP financial measures, read together with adjusted non-GAAP and other financial measures, provide readers with a better understanding of how management assesses BCE's performance.

National Instrument 52-112, Non-GAAP and Other Financial Measures Disclosure (NI 52-112), prescribes disclosure requirements that apply to the following specified financial measures:

This section provides a description and classification of the specified financial measures contemplated by NI 52-112 that we use in this news release to explain our financial results except that, for supplementary financial measures, an explanation of such measures is provided where they are first referred to in this news release if the supplementary financial measures' labelling is not sufficiently descriptive.

A non-GAAP financial measure is a financial measure used to depict our historical or expected future financial performance, financial position or cash flow and, with respect to its composition, either excludes an amount that is included in, or includes an amount that is excluded from, the composition of the most directly comparable financial measure disclosed in BCE's consolidated primary financial statements. We believe that non-GAAP financial measures are reflective of our ongoing operating results and provide readers with an understanding of management's perspective on and analysis of our performance.

Below are descriptions of the non-GAAP financial measures that we use in this news release to explain our results as well as reconciliations to the most directly comparable financial measures under IFRS Accounting Standards.

Adjusted net earnings â Adjusted net earnings is a non-GAAP financial measure and it does not have any standardized meaning under IFRS Accounting Standards. Therefore, it is unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other issuers.

We define adjusted net earnings as net earnings (loss) attributable to common shareholders before severance, acquisition and other costs, net mark-to-market losses (gains) on derivatives used to economically hedge equity settled share-based compensation plans, net equity losses (gains) on investments in associates and joint ventures, net losses (gains) on investments, net early debt redemption costs (gains), impairment of assets and discontinued operations, net of tax and NCI.

We use adjusted net earnings and we believe that certain investors and analysts use this measure, among other ones, to assess the performance of our businesses without the effects of severance, acquisition and other costs, net mark-to-market losses (gains) on derivatives used to economically hedge equity settled share-based compensation plans, net equity losses (gains) on investments in associates and joint ventures, net losses (gains) on investments, net early debt redemption costs (gains), impairment of assets and discontinued operations, net of tax and NCI. We exclude these items because they affect the comparability of our financial results and could potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding these items does not imply they are non-recurring.

The most directly comparable financial measure under IFRS Accounting Standards is net earnings (loss) attributable to common shareholders.

The following table is a reconciliation of net earnings attributable to common shareholders to adjusted net earnings on a consolidated basis.

economically hedge equity settled share-based compensation plans

Free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities â Free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities are non-GAAP financial measures and they do not have any standardized meaning under IFRS Accounting Standards. Therefore, they are unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other issuers.

In Q1 2026, we updated our definitions of free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities to exclude income taxes paid on significant divestitures included within cash flows from operating activities. This change does not impact the amounts for free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities previously presented. We exclude this item as it could affect the comparability of our financial results and potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding this item does not imply it is non-recurring.

We define free cash flow as cash flows from operating activities, excluding cash from discontinued operations, income taxes paid on significant divestitures, acquisition and other costs paid (which include significant litigation costs) and voluntary pension funding, less capital expenditures, preferred share dividends and dividends paid by subsidiaries to NCI. We exclude cash from discontinued operations, income taxes paid on significant divestitures, acquisition and other costs paid and voluntary pension funding because they affect the comparability of our financial results and could potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding these items does not imply they are non-recurring.

We define free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities as cash flows from operating activities, excluding cash from discontinued operations, income taxes paid on significant divestitures, acquisition and other costs paid (which include significant litigation costs) and voluntary pension funding, less principal payment of lease liabilities, capital expenditures, preferred share dividends and dividends paid by subsidiaries to NCI. We exclude cash from discontinued operations, income taxes paid on significant divestitures, acquisition and other costs paid and voluntary pension funding because they affect the comparability of our financial results and could potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding these items does not imply they are non-recurring.

We consider free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities to be important indicators of the financial strength and performance of our businesses. Free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities show how much cash is available to pay dividends on common shares, repay debt and reinvest in our company. We believe that certain investors and analysts use free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities to value a business and its underlying assets and to evaluate the financial strength and performance of our businesses. The most directly comparable financial measure under IFRS Accounting Standards is cash flows from operating activities.

The following table is a reconciliation of cash flows from operating activities to free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities on a consolidated basis.

The term net debt does not have any standardized meaning under IFRS Accounting Standards. Therefore, it is unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other issuers.

We define net debt as debt due within one year plus long-term debt and 50% of outstanding preferred shares, less 50% of junior subordinated debt included within long-term debt, and less cash, cash equivalents and short-term investments, as shown in BCE's statements of financial position.

We, and certain investors and analysts, consider net debt to be an important indicator of the company's financial leverage.

Net debt is calculated using several asset and liability categories from the statements of financial position. The most directly comparable financial measure under IFRS Accounting Standards is long-term debt. The following table is a reconciliation of long-term debt to net debt on a consolidated basis.

A non-GAAP ratio is a financial measure disclosed in the form of a ratio, fraction, percentage or similar representation and that has a non-GAAP financial measure as one or more of its components.

Below is a description of the non-GAAP ratio that we use in this news release to explain our results.

Adjusted EPS â Adjusted EPS is a non-GAAP ratio and it does not have any standardized meaning under IFRS Accounting Standards. Therefore, it is unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other issuers.

We define adjusted EPS as adjusted net earnings per BCE common share. Adjusted net earnings is a non-GAAP financial measure. For further details on adjusted net earnings, refer to Non-GAAP Financial Measures above.

We use adjusted EPS, and we believe that certain investors and analysts use this measure, among other ones, to assess the performance of our businesses without the effects of severance, acquisition and other costs, net mark-to-market losses (gains) on derivatives used to economically hedge equity settled share-based compensation plans, net equity losses (gains) on investments in associates and joint ventures, net losses (gains) on investments, net early debt redemption costs (gains), impairment of assets and discontinued operations, net of tax and NCI. We exclude these items because they affect the comparability of our financial results and could potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding these items does not imply they are non-recurring.

A total of segments measure is a financial measure that is a subtotal or total of 2 or more reportable segments and is disclosed within the Notes to BCE's consolidated primary financial statements.

Below is a description of the total of segments measures that we use in this news release to explain our results as well as a reconciliation to the most directly comparable financial measure under IFRS Accounting Standards.

Adjusted EBITDA and Bell CTS adjusted EBITDA â Adjusted EBITDA is a total of segments measure. We define adjusted EBITDA as operating revenues less operating costs as shown in BCE's consolidated income statements.

We define Bell CTS adjusted EBITDA as BCE adjusted EBITDA less Bell Media adjusted EBITDA.

A supplementary financial measure is a financial measure that is not reported in BCE's consolidated financial statements, and is, or is intended to be, reported periodically to represent historical or expected future financial performance, financial position, or cash flows.

An explanation of such measures is provided where they are first referred to in this news release if the supplementary financial measures' labelling is not sufficiently descriptive.

A capital management measure is a financial measure that is intended to enable a reader to evaluate our objectives, policies and processes for managing our capital and is disclosed within the Notes to BCE's consolidated financial statements.

The financial reporting framework used to prepare the financial statements requires disclosure that helps readers assess the company's capital management objectives, policies, and processes, as set out in IFRS Accounting Standards in IAS 1 â Presentation of Financial Statements. BCE has its own methods for managing capital and liquidity, and IFRS Accounting Standards do not prescribe any particular calculation method.

The net debt leverage ratio represents net debt divided by adjusted EBITDA. Net debt used in the calculation of the net debt leverage ratio is a non-GAAP financial measure. For further details on net debt, see section Non-GAAP financial measures above. For the purposes of calculating our net debt leverage ratio, adjusted EBITDA is twelve-month trailing adjusted EBITDA.

We use, and believe that certain investors and analysts use, the net debt leverage ratio as a measure of financial leverage.

the quality of our products and services and the extent to which they may be subject to defects or fail to comply with applicable government regulations and standards; and the expected timing and completion of the proposed disposition of Bell Mobility Inc.'s land mobile radio networks services business are subject to closing conditions, termination rights and other risks and uncertainties including, without limitation, relevant third-party approvals, which may affect its completion, terms or timing and, as such, there can be no assurance that the proposed disposition will occur, or that it will occur on the terms and conditions, or at the time, currently contemplated; the expected timing and completion of the proposed transaction with La Caisse are subject to closing conditions, termination rights and other risks and uncertainties, which may affect its completion, terms or timing and, as such, there can be no assurance that the proposed transaction will occur, or that it will occur on the terms and conditions, or at the time, currently contemplated, or that the potential benefits expected to result from the proposed transaction will be realized; risks relating to the development, construction and operation of data centres; data centre customer concentration and counterparty credit; demand for data centre capacity and competition; and AI and data centre regulatory, environmental and permitting matters.

Best Bitmap-to-Vector Tools for Print and Cut Workflows (2026): CorelDRAW PowerTRACE Highlighted for Faster, Cleaner Vectorization by Expert Consumers

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NEW YORK, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Expert Consumers has recognized CorelDRAW PowerTRACE in its coverage of the best bitmap-to-vector tools for print and cut workflows in 2026, highlighting improvements in tracing speed, vector quality, and production efficiency. The recognition focuses on CorelDRAW Graphics Suite 2026 and its enhanced PowerTRACE technology, which helps print shops, sign makers, apparel decorators, and graphic designers convert low-resolution images into editable, scalable vector artwork.

Bitmap-to-Vector Tools for Print and Cut Workflows (2026)

The recognition reflects a common challenge in commercial printing and graphic production. Customer-supplied artwork frequently arrives as JPEG files, PNG images, or scanned illustrations that cannot be enlarged without losing clarity. Converting these files into usable vector graphics can require extensive manual adjustments, particularly when preparing designs for cutting equipment or large-format output. CorelDRAW PowerTRACE addresses these requirements by transforming bitmap images into editable vector objects while supporting further refinement through integrated design tools.

Improved PowerTRACE Technology Simplifies Bitmap-to-Vector Conversion

Bitmap images consist of individual pixels that determine how graphics appear at a specific resolution. Although suitable for photographs and digital content, pixel-based artwork can become blurred or visibly jagged when enlarged beyond its original dimensions.

Vector graphics use mathematically defined paths rather than fixed pixels. These paths remain sharp when resized, making vector artwork suitable for applications ranging from small product labels to large storefront signage.

For print-and-cut production, accurate vector geometry is particularly important. Cutting equipment relies on defined paths to determine where material should be separated. Excessive nodes, irregular curves, or poorly structured outlines can introduce complications during production.

CorelDRAW PowerTRACE converts bitmap images into editable vector objects, providing controls for adjusting tracing detail, smoothing curves, and refining shapes.

The September 2026 update introduces improvements designed to simplify this process.

According to CorelDRAW, enhanced PowerTRACE technology delivers up to four times faster tracing performance, depending on source image complexity and tracing conditions. The improvement can reduce repetitive preparation work when converting multiple customer graphics or processing artwork revisions.

An updated tracing interface makes conversion controls more accessible, while improved object structure supports cleaner, more manageable vector results.

Enhanced color handling helps preserve important visual distinctions when tracing multicolor logos, illustrations, and branded artwork.

AI-powered background removal also supports the conversion process by helping isolate subjects from unwanted backgrounds. Removing unnecessary image elements before tracing can reduce unwanted shapes in the resulting vector artwork.

These enhancements address common production requirements without eliminating the ability to refine traced graphics manually.

Preparing Low-Resolution Artwork for Print and Cut Production

Print-and-cut workflows often begin with imperfect source files. A customer may provide a small JPEG logo for vinyl stickers, a scanned illustration for apparel printing, or a flattened graphic intended for a large promotional display.

Preparing these images for production requires more than increasing resolution.

PowerTRACE provides two tracing approaches suited to different artwork characteristics.

Outline Trace converts filled graphics, logos, and illustrations into editable shapes. Centerline Trace follows the center of existing strokes, making the method suitable for line drawings and certain technical illustrations.

Selecting the appropriate tracing method helps produce vector geometry that better reflects the intended application.

After conversion, CorelDRAW's curve-editing tools allow designers to review and adjust the resulting artwork.

Irregular edges can be smoothed, unnecessary nodes can be removed, and overlapping elements can be corrected before production.

For applications involving vinyl cutting or shaped stickers, a separate cutting outline must also be prepared.

CorelDRAW includes drawing and contour tools that support the creation of cutting paths around vector artwork. Depending on the equipment and production software, cutting paths may require specific spot-color names, such as CutContour, to identify the intended cutting boundaries.

The distinction between vectorization and cutting preparation is important. PowerTRACE converts the original image into editable vector artwork, while subsequent production steps establish the paths and settings required by cutting equipment.

Final output must also account for dimensions, material specifications, color requirements, and equipment compatibility.

By combining vector conversion with editing and contour tools, CorelDRAW supports a more connected process from supplied artwork to production preparation.

Integrated Design Tools Support Accurate Vector Editing and Printing

CorelDRAW Graphics Suite 2026 extends beyond image tracing through tools designed for professional illustration, layout, and print production.

One important capability is precision curve editing.

CorelDRAW includes BÃ©zier, freehand, polyline, and B-spline tools, alongside controls for modifying nodes and adjusting vector paths.

These features allow designers to refine traced artwork without reconstructing entire graphics. Precise adjustments are particularly useful when preparing detailed lettering, decorative patterns, technical illustrations, or intricate cutting outlines.

Color management also plays an important role in commercial production.

CorelDRAW supports CMYK color separations, soft proofing, and integrated access to Pantone Color Libraries. These capabilities help designers prepare artwork for printing and evaluate color reproduction before final output.

For signage and large-format graphics, CorelDRAW supports designing at actual scale. Artwork can be prepared for small promotional products or larger displays while maintaining editable vector geometry.

The software also includes an Export docker or inspector for managing output settings and exporting selected objects, pages, and design assets.

This flexibility can be useful when preparing multiple versions of a design for different production requirements.

Multipage layout tools further support the organization of related graphics within a single document, helping designers manage production assets and revisions.

Together, these capabilities allow bitmap conversion to function as part of a broader design and production workflow rather than an isolated operation.

CorelDRAW Graphics Suite 2026 Addresses Common Production Challenges

Preparing low-resolution logos and scanned artwork for printing often involves removing unwanted backgrounds, refining uneven edges, and creating accurate cutting paths. CorelDRAW PowerTRACE addresses these challenges through enhanced bitmap-to-vector conversion, improved object structure, and faster tracing performance.

Although automated tracing can reduce repetitive preparation work, complex graphics and small lettering may still require manual adjustments. CorelDRAW's integrated curve-editing and production tools allow designers to refine vector shapes and prepare artwork for different printing and cutting requirements.

For businesses producing signage, decals, apparel, and product labels, these capabilities support more efficient artwork preparation while maintaining control over the finished design.

The recognition by Expert Consumers highlights CorelDRAW PowerTRACE's role in simplifying bitmap-to-vector conversion and supporting the creation of clean, scalable graphics for professional print-and-cut workflows.

From November 3 to 22, 2026, CorelDRAW Graphics Suite is 31% off: $379 for perpetual license or $185 annual subscription. Regional pricing may vary.

The full review is available at the Expert Consumers website.

BCE reports third quarter 2026 results

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This news release contains forward-looking statements. For a description of the related risk factors and assumptions, please see the section entitled "Caution Regarding Forward-Looking Statements" later in this news release. The information contained in this news release is unaudited.

MONTREAL, Nov. 5, 2026 /CNW/ -- BCE Inc. (TSX: BCE) (NYSE: BCE) today reported results for the third quarter (Q3) of 2026.

"In the year since our Investor Day 2025, Bell's Q3 results show responsible investment in key growth areas and steadfast execution of our long-term priorities," said Mirko Bibic, President and CEO, BCE and Bell Canada.

Bell Business Markets remains a strong performer with revenue up 9.2%, including 41% growth in AI-powered solutions revenue. We also continued to advance Bell AI Fabric, including a non-binding MOU with the Government of Saskatchewan to explore up to 900 megawatts of additional capacity at our facility outside Regina, creating a potential path to 1.2 gigawatts of total capacity.

We also announced an agreement with La Caisse to sell a 49.9% stake in our newly formed wireless tower infrastructure company, allowing us to surface the value of our tower infrastructure assets while retaining control of the business.

Our commitment to 3.5x net debt leverage1 by the end of 2027 remains unchanged, and we remain focused on disciplined execution of our four strategic priorities. As we invest in the future to create value for shareholders, we are pleased with the progress we've made as we head into the end of 2026."

1 Adjusted EBITDA is a total of segments measure, adjusted net earnings and free cash flow are non-GAAP financial measures, adjusted EPS is a non-GAAP ratio, and net debt leverage ratio is a capital management measure. Refer to the Non-GAAP and Other Financial Measures section in this news release for more information on these measures.

2 Bell Business Markets (BBM) operating revenue includes wireline and wireless service and product revenue from large and medium retail business customers generated from the sale of communications services and AI-powered solutions. Communications revenue is comprised of: core connectivity services, including wireless, Internet, voice, and data network services sold directly to enterprise customers, as well as advanced cloud-based services such as Contact Center as a Service (CCaaS) and unified-communications-as-a-service (UCaaS). Al-powered solutions revenue is comprised of revenue from Ateko, Bell Cyber, and Bell Al Fabric.

Build a digital media and content powerhouse

5 Based on 5G/5G+ Network Performance Score, calculated by Rohde & Schwarz SwissQual AG, independent network analysts based in Switzerland, from data collected in Canada comparing data-only performance of other national wireless networks from March to June 2026. 5G+ results are defined as connectivity while using 3500MHz / 3800 MHz spectrum. ($ millions except per share amounts) (unaudited)

Net earnings attributable to common shareholders

BCE operating revenues were $6,182 million in Q3 2026, up 2.2% compared to Q3 2025. This was the result of 1.6% higher service revenue of $5,412 million and a 6.9% increase in product revenue to $770 million.

Net earnings in Q3 decreased 84.6% to $701 million and net earnings attributable to common shareholders totalled $630 million, or $0.67 per share, down 86.0% and 86.2%, respectively.

Adjusted net earnings were down 5.2% in Q3 to $695 million, resulting in a 6.3% decrease in adjusted EPS to $0.74.

Adjusted EBITDA grew 0.9% in Q3 to $2,786 million, reflecting the contribution of Bell CTS U.S. and an 8.0% increase at Bell Media, partly offset by a 0.8% decrease at Bell CTS Canada.

BCE capital expenditures in Q3 were $1,330 million, up 49.3% from $891 million last year, corresponding to a capital intensity7 of 21.5%, compared to 14.7% in Q3 2025.

BCE cash flows from operating activities in Q3 were $2,218 million, up 15.9% from $1,914 million in Q3 2025.

Free cash flow was $854 million, down 14.9% from $1,003 million in Q3 2025, mainly due to higher capital expenditures, partly offset by higher cash flows from operating activities, excluding cash from income taxes paid on significant divestitures and acquisition and other costs paid.

6 Adjusted EBITDA margin is defined as adjusted EBITDA divided by operating revenues. Refer to the Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) section in this news release for more information on adjusted EBITDA margin.

7 Capital intensity is defined as capital expenditures divided by operating revenues. Refer to the Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) section in this news release for more information on capital intensity.

On August 1, 2025, BCE completed its acquisition of Ziply Fiber and created the Bell CTS U.S. segment. The results of BCE's Canadian wireless and wireline operations are reported under Bell CTS Canada.

Bell CTS operating revenues increased 1.3% to $5,476 million in Q3 2026 compared to Q3 2025, driven by both higher service and product revenue. The increase in service revenue reflects the contribution from Bell CTS U.S., which was included for only two months in Q3 2025, partly offset by a year-over-year decline at Bell CTS Canada.

Bell CTS adjusted EBITDA8 grew 0.2% in Q3 to $2,530 million, reflecting the contribution from Bell CTS U.S., partly offset by a year-over-year decline at Bell CTS Canada. Bell CTS adjusted EBITDA margin decreased 0.5 percentage points to 46.2% from 46.7% in Q3 2025. The margin decline reflected a higher proportion of lower-margin product sales and a 2.2% increase in operating costs, mainly due to a full quarter of Ziply Fiber's operating expenses compared to two months in Q3 2025.

Bell CTS retail residential NAS net losses3 increased by 5.8% to 48,659 in Q3 2026, reflecting increased net losses at Bell CTS Canada, partly offset by fewer net losses at Bell CTS U.S.

Bell CTS' retail residential NAS customer base3,11,12 totalled 1,586,229 at the end of Q3 2026, representing a 10.2% decline compared to Q3 2025. The decrease reflects declines at both Bell CTS Canada and Bell CTS U.S.

8 Bell CTS adjusted EBITDA is a total of segments measure. Refer to the Non-GAAP and Other Financial Measures section in this news release for more information on this measure.

Bell CTS Canada operating revenue decreased 0.2% to $5,238 million in Q3 2026 compared to Q3 2025, due to lower service revenue, partly offset by higher product revenue.

Bell CTS Canada product revenue increased 6.9% in Q3 to $770 million, reflecting greater equipment sales to large business customers, including the timing of certain wireline equipment deals.

Bell CTS Canada adjusted EBITDA decreased 0.8% in Q3 to $2,435 million, reflecting the flow-through of lower year-over-year service revenue and 0.3% higher operating costs. Together with a greater proportion of lower-margin product sales, these factors contributed to a 0.3 percentage-point margin decline to 46.5% from 46.8% in Q3 2025. Mobile connected device3 net activations decreased 49.9% in Q3 2026, compared to the same period last year, mainly due to higher business Internet of Things (IoT) deactivations.

Bell CTS U.S. operating revenues were $238 million in Q3 2026, compared to $160 million in Q3 2025. The increase primarily reflects the inclusion of a full quarter of Ziply Fiber's results, compared to only two months following its acquisition on August 1, 2025. Growth in Internet revenue from the continued expansion of Ziply Fiber's FTTP footprint was offset by ongoing declines in legacy voice and data services.

Bell CTS U.S. adjusted EBITDA was $95 million in Q3 2026, compared to $71 million in Q3 2025. Operating costs were $143 million, compared to $89 million. The Q3 2025 comparatives include only two months of Ziply Fiber. Adjusted EBITDA margin was 39.9%, down 4.5 percentage points from 44.4%, reflecting acquisition-related changes to the cost base, higher payments to the Network FiberCo partnership and the flow-through of lower legacy voice and data revenue.

Advertising revenue was up 9.0% in Q3 2026, reflecting strong sports advertising performance driven by the FIFA World Cup 2026â¢ and growth in digital video advertising revenue17, partly offset by continued softness in traditional advertising demand.

Bell Media adjusted EBITDA was up 8.0% to $256 million in Q3 2026 on the flow-through of higher operating revenue. However, margin declined 0.5 percentage points to 31.9% from 32.4% in Q3 2025, reflecting a 10.5% increase in operating costs associated with the FIFA World Cup 2026â¢ and contractual rights increases for premium content, including to support the growth of Crave. These factors were partly offset by cost reduction initiatives.

BCE uses various financial measures to assess its business performance. Certain of these measures are calculated in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards or GAAP while certain other measures do not have a standardized meaning under GAAP. We believe that our GAAP financial measures, read together with adjusted non-GAAP and other financial measures, provide readers with a better understanding of how management assesses BCE's performance.

National Instrument 52-112, Non-GAAP and Other Financial Measures Disclosure (NI 52-112), prescribes disclosure requirements that apply to the following specified financial measures:

This section provides a description and classification of the specified financial measures contemplated by NI 52-112 that we use in this news release to explain our financial results except that, for supplementary financial measures, an explanation of such measures is provided where they are first referred to in this news release if the supplementary financial measures' labelling is not sufficiently descriptive.

A non-GAAP financial measure is a financial measure used to depict our historical or expected future financial performance, financial position or cash flow and, with respect to its composition, either excludes an amount that is included in, or includes an amount that is excluded from, the composition of the most directly comparable financial measure disclosed in BCE's consolidated primary financial statements. We believe that non-GAAP financial measures are reflective of our ongoing operating results and provide readers with an understanding of management's perspective on and analysis of our performance.

Below are descriptions of the non-GAAP financial measures that we use in this news release to explain our results as well as reconciliations to the most directly comparable financial measures under IFRS Accounting Standards.

Adjusted net earnings â Adjusted net earnings is a non-GAAP financial measure and it does not have any standardized meaning under IFRS Accounting Standards. Therefore, it is unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other issuers.

We define adjusted net earnings as net earnings (loss) attributable to common shareholders before severance, acquisition and other costs, net mark-to-market losses (gains) on derivatives used to economically hedge equity settled share-based compensation plans, net equity losses (gains) on investments in associates and joint ventures, net losses (gains) on investments, net early debt redemption costs (gains), impairment of assets and discontinued operations, net of tax and NCI.

We use adjusted net earnings and we believe that certain investors and analysts use this measure, among other ones, to assess the performance of our businesses without the effects of severance, acquisition and other costs, net mark-to-market losses (gains) on derivatives used to economically hedge equity settled share-based compensation plans, net equity losses (gains) on investments in associates and joint ventures, net losses (gains) on investments, net early debt redemption costs (gains), impairment of assets and discontinued operations, net of tax and NCI. We exclude these items because they affect the comparability of our financial results and could potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding these items does not imply they are non-recurring.

The most directly comparable financial measure under IFRS Accounting Standards is net earnings (loss) attributable to common shareholders.

Free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities â Free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities are non-GAAP financial measures and they do not have any standardized meaning under IFRS Accounting Standards. Therefore, they are unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other issuers.

In Q1 2026, we updated our definitions of free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities to exclude income taxes paid on significant divestitures included within cash flows from operating activities. This change does not impact the amounts for free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities previously presented. We exclude this item as it could affect the comparability of our financial results and potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding this item does not imply it is non-recurring.

We define free cash flow as cash flows from operating activities, excluding cash from discontinued operations, income taxes paid on significant divestitures, acquisition and other costs paid (which include significant litigation costs) and voluntary pension funding, less capital expenditures, preferred share dividends and dividends paid by subsidiaries to NCI. We exclude cash from discontinued operations, income taxes paid on significant divestitures, acquisition and other costs paid and voluntary pension funding because they affect the comparability of our financial results and could potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding these items does not imply they are non-recurring.

We define free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities as cash flows from operating activities, excluding cash from discontinued operations, income taxes paid on significant divestitures, acquisition and other costs paid (which include significant litigation costs) and voluntary pension funding, less principal payment of lease liabilities, capital expenditures, preferred share dividends and dividends paid by subsidiaries to NCI. We exclude cash from discontinued operations, income taxes paid on significant divestitures, acquisition and other costs paid and voluntary pension funding because they affect the comparability of our financial results and could potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding these items does not imply they are non-recurring.

We consider free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities to be important indicators of the financial strength and performance of our businesses. Free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities show how much cash is available to pay dividends on common shares, repay debt and reinvest in our company. We believe that certain investors and analysts use free cash flow and free cash flow after payment of lease liabilities to value a business and its underlying assets and to evaluate the financial strength and performance of our businesses. The most directly comparable financial measure under IFRS Accounting Standards is cash flows from operating activities.

The term net debt does not have any standardized meaning under IFRS Accounting Standards. Therefore, it is unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other issuers.

We define net debt as debt due within one year plus long-term debt and 50% of outstanding preferred shares, less 50% of junior subordinated debt included within long-term debt, and less cash, cash equivalents and short-term investments, as shown in BCE's statements of financial position.

We, and certain investors and analysts, consider net debt to be an important indicator of the company's financial leverage.

Net debt is calculated using several asset and liability categories from the statements of financial position. Adjusted EPS â Adjusted EPS is a non-GAAP ratio and it does not have any standardized meaning under IFRS Accounting Standards. Therefore, it is unlikely to be comparable to similar measures presented by other issuers.

We define adjusted EPS as adjusted net earnings per BCE common share. Adjusted net earnings is a non-GAAP financial measure. For further details on adjusted net earnings, refer to Non-GAAP Financial Measures above.

We use adjusted EPS, and we believe that certain investors and analysts use this measure, among other ones, to assess the performance of our businesses without the effects of severance, acquisition and other costs, net mark-to-market losses (gains) on derivatives used to economically hedge equity settled share-based compensation plans, net equity losses (gains) on investments in associates and joint ventures, net losses (gains) on investments, net early debt redemption costs (gains), impairment of assets and discontinued operations, net of tax and NCI. We exclude these items because they affect the comparability of our financial results and could potentially distort the analysis of trends in business performance. Excluding these items does not imply they are non-recurring.

A supplementary financial measure is a financial measure that is not reported in BCE's consolidated financial statements, and is, or is intended to be, reported periodically to represent historical or expected future financial performance, financial position, or cash flows.

An explanation of such measures is provided where they are first referred to in this news release if the supplementary financial measures' labelling is not sufficiently descriptive.

A capital management measure is a financial measure that is intended to enable a reader to evaluate our objectives, policies and processes for managing our capital and is disclosed within the Notes to BCE's consolidated financial statements.

The financial reporting framework used to prepare the financial statements requires disclosure that helps readers assess the company's capital management objectives, policies, and processes, as set out in IFRS Accounting Standards in IAS 1 â Presentation of Financial Statements. BCE has its own methods for managing capital and liquidity, and IFRS Accounting Standards do not prescribe any particular calculation method.

The net debt leverage ratio represents net debt divided by adjusted EBITDA. Net debt used in the calculation of the net debt leverage ratio is a non-GAAP financial measure. For further details on net debt, see section Non-GAAP financial measures above. For the purposes of calculating our net debt leverage ratio, adjusted EBITDA is twelve-month trailing adjusted EBITDA.

We use, and believe that certain investors and analysts use, the net debt leverage ratio as a measure of financial leverage.

NTT DATA earns recognition from SAP as client-adopted AI agents create real business impact

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BIELEFELD, Germany, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Two SAP recognitions underscore the expertise of NTT DATA, a global leader in AI, digital business and technology services, in bringing agentic AI into real-world business processes and scaling SAP Business AI Platform across client environments. At the Partner Summit at SAP TechEd Berlin in late October, the company successfully participated in the partner agent race to SAP TechEd and received SAP Business AI Platform Validated Partner designation.

The recognitions acknowledge NTT DATA's strong capabilities in developing and deploying a growing portfolio of AI agents that deliver measurable results for clients. At Lemvigh-MÃ¼ller, the Purchase Order Confirmation Matching Agent enables more than 90% of supplier confirmations to be automated, with approximately 98% matching accuracy. The Project Staffing Agent deployed for zeb has helped reduce the time needed for individual entries by 50%. Synthomer uses the Purchase Order Conversion Agent to bring agentic AI into its order-to-close processes. For order intake, beta tests have achieved a 98% first-time material match rate.

These real-world implementations demonstrate how NTT DATA is pursuing its ambition to become the world's leading AI-native services company and helping turn SAP's vision of the Autonomous Enterprise into business reality.

"The real promise of agentic AI begins when it leaves the pilot stage and becomes part of everyday business," says Krunal Patel, Global AI, Innovation & Software Assets Leader at NTT DATA Business Solutions. "Our teams have put tremendous effort into translating potential into practical solutions that tackle real operational challenges. The recognition at SAP TechEd is a testament to that dedication. Just as importantly, it motivates us to keep moving forward, pushing the boundaries of what is possible with SAP Business AI Platform while keeping client value firmly at the center of what we do."

The developer has assisted on almost every mainline Zelda since Skyward Sword

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Xenoblade developer Monolith Soft has confirmed that it was involved in the development of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake.

Monolith Soft has been majorly involved in the development of Zelda titles over the last 15 years, so much so that Daiki Iwamoto, general manager for the Zelda series, said he'd like to see the studio lead its own Zelda game. And Monolith Soft's previous efforts with the series led to speculation that Ocarina of Time remake would be developed by the studio – especially when it's adept at making graphically intensive titles for their given systems – similar to how Grezzo developed Zelda remakes before creating its own unique title with Echoes of Wisdom.

It looked like this would be the case when a Norwegian retailer listed the studio as the remake's developer, but it was since revealed that development of the remake was happening internally at Nintendo. However, upon the release of the game, the Monolith Soft Twitter account revealed that the studio was involved in the development, with it citing game design, programming, and graphics roles on the title.

【お知らせ】 モノリスソフトがゲームデザイン、プログラミング、グラフィックの部分受託開発を行った「ゼルダの伝説 時のオカリナ」が任天堂株式会社より発売されました。 #時のオカリナ https://t.co/voh5RIO516November 5, 2026

While the studio has been most well-known for creating and developing the Xenoblade Chronicles games over the last decade and a half, Monolith Soft has been one of the biggest MVPs in Nintendo history. Since 2008 with Super Smash Bros. Brawl, Monolith Soft has helped develop a ton of Nintendo's biggest titles, including Mario Kart World, every Splatoon game, and Animal Crossing: New Horizons.

But its biggest contribution has been to The Legend of Zelda, where the studio assisted with Skyward Sword, A Link Between Worlds, Breath of the Wild, and Tears of the Kingdom. I do hope that Iwamoto's wish comes to fruition, because having Grezzo handling 2D Zelda and Nintendo handling the open-world titles, it'd be great to have a third studio handling development of more traditional 3D Zeldas in the vein of Ocarina of Time.

Check out our The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time review to see why it's the highest-rated game of 2026. You can also read our Legend of Zelda Switch 2 stock live blog if you're keen to grab one of Nintendo's fancy new consoles for the game's launch.

Bitcoin Mining Giant MARA Sparks Selling Concerns With $133 Million Transfer

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin mining behemoth MARA Holdings has again raised concerns over possible selling pressure on Bitcoin after it transferred around $133 million worth of BTC to multiple institutional crypto platforms.

According to data from blockchain analytics firm Lookonchain, MARA moved a total of 1,650 BTC (around $132.8 million) to platforms like Galaxy Digital, Copper, and others in the past 12 hours.

The latest transactions, which were first reported on Nov. 5, came a few weeks after a series of massive Bitcoin movements involving the publicly listed mining company.

It seems MARA is once again dumping Bitcoin, referencing recent transfers of massive amounts of BTC to trading platforms and custodians.

Nevertheless, it is possible that the transfers do not actually indicate that MARA has sold BTC.

It's worth noting that platforms like Galaxy Digital and Copper offer different services, including trading and custody, for institutional clients.

So it could be that MARA moved the assets for liquidity, collateral, or other purposes.

It's worth mentioning that the latest BTC movements come after a few similar transactions involving MARA throughout October.

On Oct. 9, MARA moved around 996 BTC ($81.13 million at that time) to a wallet linked to crypto platform Galaxy Digital.

The transaction prompted some speculation about possible BTC selling, though it didn't confirm whether Galaxy Digital actually liquidated the assets or not.

On Oct. 19, Lookonchain again identified some other transactions involving wallets linked to the Bitcoin miner.

As per the analytics firm's data, the wallets linked to the Bitcoin miner sent 1,000 BTC (approximately $82.8 million) to crypto exchanges like Binance.

Towards the end of October, concerns over possible selling increased.

Specifically, on Oct. 27, MARA moved another 1,000 BTC (around $80.19 million) to institutional crypto custody platform BitGo.

The following day, Lookonchain noted another transaction of 1,000 BTC, worth about $80.66 million, to BitGo.

As per its second-quarter financial disclosure, MARA sold around 23,093 BTC for $1.6 billion in the first half of 2026.

In the second quarter alone, MARA sold 2,213 BTC at an average price of $73,078.

Meanwhile, MARA has also made it clear that it is no longer interested in holding all the Bitcoin it mines.

Having tested Ooni’s Halo Core stand mixer, which is perfect for making pizza dough, I’ve been eating my way through a fair few pies. But I still haven’t invested in an indoor pizza oven to get the perfect bake. Now I think that’s about to change.

Ahead of Black Friday, I’ve spotted an epic deal on the Chefman Indoor Pizza Oven — it’s now $199 at Amazon, an epic 60% saving before the big sales event has even started.

One word of advice, I wouldn’t wait until Black Friday, because I doubt Chefman’s Indoor Pizza Oven will be around for long at this amazing price.

Chefman's sleek indoor pizza oven is perfectly compact to fit on a kitchen countertop and measures 18.7 (D) x 17 (W) x 10.94 (H) inches. It has five touchscreen presets to cook 12-inch pizzas in minutes, heating up to 800°F. Plus, you get a pizza stone, pizza peel and a digital cookbook.

We test a whole load of outdoor pizza ovens at Tom’s Guide, but right now, I’m happy to bake my pies inside. And I think most pizza lovers will come to the same conclusion once the temperature drops.

What’s more, unlike an outdoor pizza oven, you don’t have to worry about what fuel to use; you can simply plug the Chefman Indoor Pizza Oven into your electric.

How do you like your pizza? You can choose from Neapolitan, New York, Thin-Crust, or, if you prefer to go wild, you can customize your bake with the manual setting. Plus, there’s a frozen setting for when you don’t have time to make your pizza from scratch.

With two heating elements that reach up to 800°F for one hot pie, the oven retains heat for back-to-back cooking — perfect for pizza parties and hungry families. The double-paned window also gives you a clear view of what’s happening inside, so you can check if your cheese is bubbling or your crust is crisping.

If you love pizza or know someone who does, you really can't turn down this 60% discount.

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bukayo Saka is ‘a bit fragile’ and must work on preventing injury problems in future, reckons former Arsenal defender Mikael Silvestre.

The Gunners winger is currently sidelined with a hamstring problem picked up in a Champions League clash with Bayern Munich on October 21.

Saka is not expected to be back in action until December, with Noni Madueke filling in on the right flank in his absence.

The 25-year-old has been hampered by some injury issues over the last two seasons, but has still managed to rack up a huge amount of appearances, 323 for Arsenal in total.

He played 49 times in all competitions last season, although the 2024/25 campaign saw him play the least since he broke into the first team, turning out 37 times.

Silvestre accepts that injuries are bound to occur for players of Saka’s ilk, but suggests he is not quite as robust as he could be.

‘When you are an explosive player like Saka, obviously the muscles are what will trouble you when you are doing those sprints all the time,’ Silvestre told PariuriX.com. ‘Maybe, he is a bit fragile, and with the amount of games things like this can happen.

‘What is important for him is the recovery and the prevention, because it’s very important for him that it doesn’t happen too often, because he is very important for Arsenal.’

Silvestre sees Madueke as an improving force in Saka’s absence, although there is still room to become sharper in the final third.

‘But it’s important to get those wins against teams who are also potentially in the race, and to keep your distance from them.

‘The positives are the defensive side of things, the 3 points, and the fact that it was at Anfield. It breaks that negative dynamic but I think it’s just for the stats really.

‘I think you need your best players at the key moments featuring in the semi-finals and the final of competitions. Arsenal need to keep Timber fit, Saliba is coming back also. You need your strong 11 and the best defence once you reach the final. So it’s still a long way away but they defend well, as a unit for sure.’

Grok AI Predicts TRUMP Crypto to Zero as Democrats Turn the US Blue

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elon Musk’s Grok AI predicts a price target for the TRUMP crypto memecoin that would see the President’s token utterly worthless

The Official TRUMP memecoin is positioned for a severe and sustained collapse, Elon Musk’s Grok AI predicts. In the 2026 midterms, Democrats delivered a sweeping victory, reclaiming control of both the House and the Senate. The party flipped multiple Republican-held Senate seats, including in traditionally red or competitive states, and secured a clear House majority.

This outcome sharply rebukes the current Republican administration and significantly weakens the party’s legislative influence for the remainder of the presidential term. With Democrats now holding both chambers, Republicans’ ability to advance their agenda is heavily constrained, and the balance of power in Washington has shifted decisively.

(SOURCE: Grok AI Predicts a Catastrophic TRUMP Crypto Crash)

Democratic majorities have historically favored stronger regulatory oversight of digital assets. A more assertive stance on crypto, ranging from expanded enforcement to potentially restrictive legislation, would remove much of the political tailwind that previously supported Trump-branded tokens.

Political memecoins like TRUMP are especially vulnerable: their value is tightly linked to narrative momentum and perceived political relevance. A cooler regulatory climate and diminished Republican influence could accelerate capital flight from these assets and pressure the more speculative corners of the broader crypto market.

TRUMP has already experienced a dramatic drawdown from its early 2025 peak near $75 and currently trades at a small fraction of that level, down -15% on the day and -98% from that $75 all-time high.

Price action shows persistent lower highs, weak follow-through on any short-term rallies, and elevated sensitivity to political headlines, sparking fear among the remaining TRUMP crypto holders.

Volume has tended to expand more on downside moves than on recoveries, a classic sign of distribution. With a large portion of the supply still subject to unlocks and concentrated holdings, the technical structure remains fragile.

A break of recent support levels would likely trigger further liquidation and accelerate the downtrend. With reduced political relevance, rising regulatory pressure, and deteriorating technicals, the path of least resistance points sharply lower.

A Democratic-led Congress is more likely to favor stricter oversight of digital assets. This environment reduces political support for crypto-friendly initiatives and raises the prospect of tighter regulation or enforcement.

Among all crypto assets, the Official TRUMP memecoin stands out as uniquely exposed. Its value is closely tied to political momentum and the current administration’s standing.

As the most visible Trump-branded token, it is expected to attract disproportionate attention from regulators, critics, and risk-averse investors. While the broader market may feel some pressure, TRUMP is positioned to suffer more severe and prolonged selling than most other tokens.

By January 1, 2027, Official TRUMP could trade in the $0.08 – $0.25 range, with a severe downside scenario testing levels below $0.05.

Awesome repositories are no longer awesome

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On GitHub, you will find a lot of repositories with the prefix 'awesome'. They usually contain curated knowledge and resources related to a certain domain, such as awesome-python (see, e.g., this repository for a list of awesome repositories). I used to see these awesome repositories as potential goldmines of good content. However, nowadays I see most of them as irrelevant and useless. There are two reasons for this, and they are both related to AI.

First, as many of these awesome lists get older, they contain a lot of outdated material if they are not actively maintained. You are in most cases better off simply asking a decent AI model for inspiration or the material you need. That is, I find it difficult to believe that most of these awesome lists are of interest to people anymore. If they remain useful, it will mostly be for agents looking to find and check relevant resources.

I had a quick look at the awesome-R repository. The repository was last updated more than a year ago, and most of the resources included in the overview are either outdated or will not be of general interest to people using R. One of the first links in the repository is an overview of the top 50 downloaded packages - as of 2016! This is simply not a good resource if you want to explore R in any shape or form.

Second, a lot of these awesome repositories are being flooded with AI slop. Go to any random awesome repository these days and you will most likely find a lot of pull requests suggesting the most generic tools and resources. For example, take a look at this awesome-free-online-tools repository. It is a small, unknown repository with 13 stars but 91 open pull requests at the time of writing. I did not look at all of these pull requests, but the ones I looked at all suggested generic vibe-coded tools.

I found this resource because of a pull request made to one of my repositories, a simple project where I store material I like related to statistics. The suggestion was to add StatOrigin ("a free, open database of industry statistics"), and once I had a quick look at the resource and the other pull requests made by the user, I could easily close the PR. This user alone has created at least six vibe-coded websites (StatOrigin, ScanChess, Sticker Printer Hub, QuizQuip, LiquidityPools, DefiLPKit) and submitted more than 100 pull requests to various awesome lists to add these websites.

It did not take me long to find examples where these tools were merged into the relevant repositories (see, e.g., here, here, and here). However, I also found examples where people did the basic work of checking out the material, such as here, where the PR was closed with the following explanation: "There is not yet sufficient evidence of qualified educator review, independent validation of the educational content, or demonstrated learning outcomes." Good stuff!

My point is not that these awesome repositories are now all bad. They can still be awesome. But as a general approach to finding good resources, awesome lists are less awesome than they used to be. As AI gets better at finding and creating resources (and creating pull requests for said resources), these awesome repositories will become less relevant and less useful.
